such that
f(x)\geq c_{0}
for all
x\in[0,1]^{d}
and suppose that

n\geq 36(M+1)^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d+2}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2}\log\biggl(\frac{(M+1)2^{d+2}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr).

(15)

Then there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\{(\check{\theta}_{h}^{M}-\theta_{h}^{*,M})^{2}\}\leq\frac{\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}^{+}}n_{S}^{2}}\biggl(\frac{C2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2M+2}.

This result is proved by studying the stochastic error of our density estimators and using techniques from the theory of
U
-statistics. This does not require any smoothness conditions on
a
or
f
, though we do assume that
f
is bounded below by a positive constant. The functions
k_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}
introduced above are the kernels of degenerate
U
-statistics considered in this proof. The degeneracy is seen by noting that
\mathbb{E}\{k_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},X)\}=0
for any
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which will mean that these
U
-statistics can be approximated by sample means.

We next study the behaviour of
\theta_{h}^{*,M}
for small values of
h
, which will naturally require smoothness assumptions on
a
and
f
; we use those introduced in (
13
) and (
14
) above. We will thus see that we can approximate
\theta_{h}^{*,M}
by
\theta^{*,(M)}
defined in (
12
) and taking the form

\theta^{*,(M)}=\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{CC}}+\sum_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}}b_{M,\eta}(m)\biggl\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X_{i})-\frac{1}{n_{S_{m}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S_{m}}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X_{S_{m},i})\biggr\}.

when
r_{S}\equiv 1
for all
S\in\mathbb{S}
.

Proposition 23
.

Let
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in(0,1]
and
L_{1},L_{2},c_{0},C_{0}>0
and suppose that
a
and
f
satisfy (B1)(
\beta_{1},L_{1}
), (B2)(
\beta_{2},L_{2}
) and (B3)(
c_{0},C_{0}
), suppose that (
15
) holds and that
|n\lambda_{S}/n_{S}-1|\leq 1/n
for all
S\in\mathbb{S}
. Then

\mathbb{E}\{(\theta_{h}^{*,M}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\}\leq\frac{72M^{4}}{\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}^{+}}n_{S}}\biggl(\frac{2^{d+1/2}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{2M}\{C_{0}^{2}L_{1}^{2}h^{2\beta_{1}}+(L_{2}^{2}h^{2\beta_{2}}+C_{0}dh)\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}+n^{-2}\}.

The proof of this result relies on the approximation
v_{\mathbf{S}}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(x)\approx\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x)
that we will formalise in (
93
) under our smoothness assumptions.

Appendix B
Additional simulation results for MICE

In this section we present further simulation results to investigate the poor performance of the imputation-based estimator in Section
4.4
. We consider the same data-generating mechanisms as previously, but restrict attention to the
n=200
settings. Here we break down the mean squared error into squared bias and variance. The best performance is in Setting (i), where there is no bias and variance is roughly constant apart from a small increase when the covariates are singular. There is a bias in Setting (ii) that grows with
\mu
. In the extreme case that
\mu=0
the response variable is independent of the covariates and we see a large variance. In Setting (iii), the dominant contribution is from the bias; we omit the point from
\rho=1
for the readability of the plot.

Figure 2:
Settings (i)–(iii) are shown from left to right. Black lines show total rescaled mean squared error, while red lines show the contribution of the squared bias and green lines show that of the variance. Error bars show three standard deviations.

Appendix C
Non-linear functionals

In this section we demonstrate that our framework extends to cover a much larger range of statistical tasks. We present three classes of examples, in each case providing an expression for the local asymptotic minimax lower bound and a sketch of the argument used to derive it. These are given in terms of generalised ANOVA decompositions of appropriate influence functions, which in these cases depend on unknown quantities. Following similar cross-fitting procedures to those used in Sections
4
and
5.1
it would be possible to construct efficient estimators; for the sake of brevity we focus on the lower bounds in this section.

C.1
Smooth functionals

Suppose that we are given data generated according to the basic model described in the introduction and wish to estimate functionals of the form

\theta(f)=\int\psi(x,f(x))\,dx

where
\psi
is a smooth, known function. This includes the quadratic functional
\int f^{2}
and the Shannon entropy
\int f\log(1/f)
, as well as other entropies, and has been studied as a class of estimands in
Laurent (1996)
, for example. As the model for the data is unchanged, the same arguments as in Section
3
allow us to demonstrate the local asymptotic normality of our sequence of experiments with respect to the inner product derived in (
9
). Now however, writing
\dot{\psi}(x,u)=(\partial\psi/\partial u)(x,u)
, we must find the adjoint of the linear map
\dot{\kappa}:h\mapsto\int f\dot{\psi}h
. It follows from our previous work, replacing
a
by
\dot{\psi}
, that we will have an efficiency bound given by

\inf_{\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}}\biggl\{\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\dot{\psi}\bigl(X,f(X)\bigr)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}(X_{S})\biggr)+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}(X_{S})^{2}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggr]\biggr\},

(16)

generalising Theorem
8
beyond the case that
\psi(x,u)=a(x)u
.

C.2
U
-statistic targets

Another commonly-studied class of estimands we can treat with our framework is that of the expected values of
U
-statistics. For
K\in\mathbb{N}
and a (known) function
a:(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{K}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, consider

\theta(f)=\mathbb{E}\{a(X_{1},\ldots,X_{K})\}=\int\ldots\int a(x_{1},\ldots,x_{K})f(x_{1})\ldots f(x_{K})\,dx_{1}\,\ldots\,dx_{K}.

This includes variances
\mathbb{E}\{\|X_{1}-X_{2}\|^{2}/2\}=\mathrm{tr}\,\mathrm{Cov}(X)
, goodness-of-fit statistics such as the kernel maximum mean discrepancy and measures of dependence such as Kendall’s
\tau
, and has been previously studied in the semi-supervised learning setting by
Cannings and Fan (2022)
and
Kim et al. (2024)
. Writing
a_{\ell}(x)=\mathbb{E}\{a(X_{1},\ldots,X_{K})|X_{\ell}=x\}
, it is straightforward to see that for such functionals we have
n^{1/2}\{\theta(f_{n^{-1/2}h})-\theta(f)\}\rightarrow\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}\int fa_{\ell}h
. It therefore follows that the efficiency bound here is given by

\inf_{\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}}\biggl\{\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}a_{\ell}(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}(X_{S})\biggr)+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}(X_{S})^{2}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggr]\biggr\},

when this is positive. Similarly to (
16
) in the previous section, we have the generalised ANOVA decomposition of the usual efficient influence function.

C.3
Two-sample functionals

In statistical analyses involving the comparison of distinct populations, such as two-sample testing and the estimation of treatment effects, it is common to estimate functionals of several distributions. Suppose that, as well as the data in our basic model, we have access to independent data with a similar structure on a second distribution of interest. For a density function
g
on
\mathbb{R}^{d^{\prime}}
for some
d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, a collection of subsets
\mathbb{T}\subseteq 2^{[d^{\prime}]}
, a collection of known distributional shifts
(s_{T}:T\in\mathbb{T})
and sample sizes
m
and
(m_{T}:T\in\mathbb{T})
, suppose that we observe

Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}g\quad\text{and}\quad Y_{T,1},\ldots,Y_{T,n_{T}}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\bar{s}_{T}g_{T}\text{ for }T\in\mathbb{T},

where
g_{T}
is the marginal density of
g
on
\mathbb{R}^{T}
and
\bar{s}_{T}(y_{T})=s_{T}(y_{T})/\int s_{T}g
is a normalised shift function. Simple functionals of interest have the form
\theta(f,g)=\mathbb{E}\{a(X,Y)\}
for a known function
a
. Taking
a(x,y)=\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{1}\leq y_{1}\}}
, for example, gives the Mann–Whitney
U
statistic for a difference in location between the first components of
X
and
Y
.

We can establish local asymptotic minimax theory by perturbing of
f
and
g
to densities proportional to
k(n^{-1/2}h(x))f(x)
and
k(n^{-1/2}\ell(x))g(x)
, respectively, where we recall the definition of
k
from Section
3.2
and where
h,\ell\in L^{2}
have mean zero. Since the datasets are independent, local asymptotic normality follows from earlier calculations with inner product

\displaystyle\langle(h,\ell),(h^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})\rangle_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}},\mu_{\mathbb{T}}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{h(X)h^{\prime}(X)\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\Cov\bigl(h_{S}(X_{S,1}),h_{S}^{\prime}(X_{S,1})\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\tau\mathbb{E}\{\ell(Y)\ell^{\prime}(Y)\}+\tau\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}}\mu_{S}\Cov\bigl(\ell_{T}(Y_{T,1}),\ell_{T}^{\prime}(Y_{T,1})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\langle h,h^{\prime}\rangle_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}+\tau\langle\ell,\ell^{\prime}\rangle_{\mu_{\mathbb{T}}},

where we write
\mu_{T}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}m_{T}/m
and assume that
m/n\rightarrow\tau\in(0,\infty)
. As in Section
C.2
we see that the derivative of
\theta
with respect to these perturbations is given by
\dot{\kappa}:(h,\ell)\mapsto\int a_{X}hf+\int a_{Y}\ell g
, where
a_{X}(x)=\mathbb{E}\{a(x,Y)\}
and
a_{Y}(y)=\mathbb{E}\{a(X,y)\}
. Using the work of Section
3
we may therefore calculate a lower bound by writing

\displaystyle\sup

\displaystyle\biggl\{\dot{\kappa}(h,\ell):\|(h,\ell)\|_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}},\mu_{\mathbb{T}}}\leq 1\biggr\}=\sup\biggl\{\int a_{X}hf+\int a_{Y}\ell g:\|h\|_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}+\tau\|\ell\|_{\mu_{\mathbb{T}}}^{2}\leq 1\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\biggl[\sup\biggl\{\biggl(\int a_{X}hf\biggr)^{2}:\|h\|_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}\leq 1\biggr\}+\frac{1}{\tau}\sup\biggl\{\biggl(\int a_{Y}\ell g\biggr)^{2}:\|\ell\|_{\mu_{\mathbb{T}}}\leq 1\biggr\}\biggr]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\alpha_{\mathbb{S}},\beta_{\mathbb{T}}}\biggl\{\mathrm{Var}\biggl(a_{X}(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}(X_{S})\biggr)+\frac{1}{\tau}\mathrm{Var}\biggl(a_{Y}(Y)-\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}}\beta_{T}(Y_{T})\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}(X_{S})^{2}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggr]+\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\beta_{T}(Y_{T})^{2}}{\tau\mu_{T}\bar{s}_{T}(Y_{T})}\biggr]\biggr\}^{1/2}.

This shows that the minimal variance in this problem is found by performing two separate generalised ANOVA decompositions.

Appendix D
Proofs of main results

D.1
Proofs for Section
3

Proof of Proposition
6
.

To simplify notation, we will sometimes write, for example,
\alpha_{S}
for the random variable
\alpha_{S}(X_{S})
when this is clear from context. Since
\mathcal{L}(0)=\mathrm{Var}\{a(X)\}<\infty
and we aim to minimise
\mathcal{L}
, we may restrict attention to
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}
such that
\alpha_{S}/\bar{r}_{S}^{1/2}\in L^{2}
for all
S\in\mathbb{S}
.

We first prove the existence of a minimiser. By elementary quadratic expansions we see that for
\mu\in[0,1]
and
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}},\beta_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\mu\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+(1-\mu)\beta_{\mathbb{S}})

\displaystyle=\Var\biggl(\mu\biggl\{a(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}(X_{S})\biggr\}+(1-\mu)\biggl\{a(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\beta_{S}(X_{S})\biggr\}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 250.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\{\mu\alpha_{S}+(1-\mu)\beta_{S}\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mu\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})+(1-\mu)\mathcal{L}(\beta_{\mathbb{S}})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\mu(1-\mu)\biggl\{2\mathrm{Cov}\biggl(a-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S},a-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\beta_{S}\biggr)+2\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{f_{S}\alpha_{S}\beta_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})-\mathcal{L}(\beta_{\mathbb{S}})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\mu\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})+(1-\mu)\mathcal{L}(\beta_{\mathbb{S}})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-\mu(1-\mu)\biggl\{\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\beta_{S}\biggr)+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\alpha_{S}-\beta_{S})^{2}\biggr\}

(17)

\displaystyle\leq\mu\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})+(1-\mu)\mathcal{L}(\beta_{\mathbb{S}})

so that
\mathcal{L}
is convex. Now let
(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)}:t\in\mathbb{N})
be a sequence of elements of
H_{\mathbb{S}}
such that
\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)})\rightarrow\inf_{\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. By the convexity of
\mathcal{L}
we have that
\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(s)}/2+\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)}/2)\leq(1/2)\{\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(s)})+\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)})\}\rightarrow\inf_{\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})
as
s,t\rightarrow\infty
, and hence that
\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(s)}/2+\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)}/2)\rightarrow 0
as
s,t\rightarrow\infty
. However, we see by (
17
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)})-\mathcal{L}\biggl(\frac{\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(s)}+\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)}}{2}\biggr)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)})+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(s)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\Var\biggl(\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}(\alpha_{S}^{(s)}-\alpha_{S}^{(t)})\biggr)+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\alpha_{S}^{(t)}-\alpha_{S}^{(s)})^{2}.

As we know that the left-hand side of this equation converges to zero as
s,t\rightarrow\infty
, and we know that
\lambda_{S}<\infty
for all
S\in\mathbb{S}
, it must be that
\int f_{S}(\alpha_{S}^{(t)}-\alpha_{S}^{(s)})^{2}/\bar{r}_{S}\rightarrow 0
for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
. Since
\|\bar{r}_{S}\|_{\infty}<\infty
we also know that
\int f_{S}(\alpha_{S}^{(t)}-\alpha_{S}^{(s)})^{2}\rightarrow 0
for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
. Thus,
\alpha_{S}^{(t)}/\bar{r}_{S}^{1/2}
and
\alpha_{S}^{(t)}
define Cauchy sequences in
H_{S}
for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
. By the completeness of these spaces there exists
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
such that
\alpha_{S}^{(t)}\rightarrow\alpha_{S}^{*}
and
\alpha_{S}^{(t)}/\bar{r}_{S}^{1/2}\rightarrow\alpha_{S}^{*}/\bar{r}_{S}^{1/2}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
, where the boundedness of
\bar{r}_{S}
is used to see that the same
\alpha_{S}^{*}
appears in both limits. Now by the continuity of
\mathcal{L}
it is clear that
\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(t)})
, so that
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
is a minimiser of
\mathcal{L}
.

We now justify the uniqueness of
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
. Suppose that
\beta_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
is another minimiser. Then by (
17
) we have

\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\leq\mathcal{L}\Bigl(\frac{\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}+\beta_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}}{2}\Bigr)\leq\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\alpha_{S}^{*}-\beta_{S}^{*})^{2},

which implies that
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}=\beta_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7
.

We start by giving a useful characterisation of minimisers of
\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})
. Specialising (
25
) and (
26
) to the
r_{S}\equiv 1
case we see that
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}
is a minimiser of
\mathcal{L}
if and only if

\mathbb{E}\biggl\{a(X)-\theta-\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}\setminus\{S\}}\alpha_{S^{\prime}}(X_{S^{\prime}})\biggm|X_{S}\biggr\}=(1+1/\lambda_{S})\alpha_{S}(X_{S})

(18)

almost surely, for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
. We prove our result by checking these stationarity conditions at our claimed minimiser defined in (
8
). Throughout the proof we use the shorthand
a_{T}
and
\tilde{a}_{T}
for
a_{T}(X_{T})
and
\tilde{a}_{T}(X_{T})
, respectively, when it does not cause confusion.

We begin by proving that
\mathbb{E}(\tilde{a}_{T}|X_{U})=0
for all
U\subset T
. This is related to standard ANOVA decompositions, and is well known, but we include a short inductive proof for completeness. In the base cases
T=\{j\}
we simply have
\tilde{a}_{T}=a_{\{j\}}-\theta
and clearly
\mathbb{E}(\tilde{a}_{T}|X_{\emptyset})=\mathbb{E}(\tilde{a}_{T})=0
. For the induction step, using the independence of the entries of
X
and the induction hypothesis, we have for any
T\subseteq[d]
and
U\subset T
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\tilde{a}_{T}|X_{U})=\mathbb{E}\biggl(a_{T}-\sum_{U^{\prime}\subset T}\tilde{a}_{U^{\prime}}\biggm|X_{U}\biggr)

\displaystyle=a_{U}-\sum_{U^{\prime}\subset T}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{a}_{U^{\prime}}|X_{U\cap U^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=a_{U}-\sum_{U^{\prime}\subseteq U}\tilde{a}_{U^{\prime}}=\tilde{a}_{U}-\tilde{a}_{U}=0,

as claimed.

We now proceed to check that the
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}
defined in (
8
) satisfies (
18
). For each
S\in\mathbb{S}
we have

\displaystyle(1+1/\lambda_{S})\alpha_{S}+\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\mathbb{E}(\alpha_{S^{\prime}}|X_{S})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{S}}\sum_{\emptyset\neq T\subseteq S}\frac{\lambda_{S}}{1+\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}:T\subseteq S^{\prime}}\lambda_{S^{\prime}}}\tilde{a}_{T}+\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{\emptyset\neq T\subseteq S^{\prime}}\frac{\lambda_{S^{\prime}}}{1+\sum_{S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{S}:T\subseteq S^{\prime\prime}}\lambda_{S^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{a}_{T}|X_{S})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda_{S}}\sum_{\emptyset\neq T\subseteq S}\frac{\lambda_{S}}{1+\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}:T\subseteq S^{\prime}}\lambda_{S^{\prime}}}\tilde{a}_{T}+\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{\emptyset\neq T\subseteq S\cap S^{\prime}}\frac{\lambda_{S^{\prime}}}{1+\sum_{S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{S}:T\subseteq S^{\prime\prime}}\lambda_{S^{\prime\prime}}}\tilde{a}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\emptyset\neq T\subseteq S}\frac{1}{1+\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}:T\subseteq S^{\prime}}\lambda_{S^{\prime}}}\tilde{a}_{T}+\sum_{\emptyset\neq T\subseteq S}\frac{\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}:T\subseteq S^{\prime}}\lambda_{S^{\prime}}}{1+\sum_{S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{S}:T\subseteq S^{\prime\prime}}\lambda_{S^{\prime\prime}}}\tilde{a}_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\emptyset\neq T\subseteq S}\tilde{a}_{T}=a_{S}-\theta,

as required.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8
.

To prove the result we will appeal to Theorem 3.11.5 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996)
. Our one-dimensional submodel is indexed by
t\in\mathbb{R}
; we will use the shorthand
f_{t}
for
f_{t\alpha^{*}}
and write
P_{n,t}
for the distribution of our data when the density for the complete sample is
f_{n^{-1/2}t}
. Our inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}
on
\mathbb{R}
will be defined by

\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}=t_{1}t_{2}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathrm{Var}\bigl((\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)\biggr],

where, given
\alpha\in L^{2}
, we write
(\alpha)_{S}(x_{S})=\mathbb{E}\{\alpha(X)|X_{S}=x_{S}\}
.

We will require control on the behaviour of
f_{t}
for small
t
. Define the normalising constant
c(t)=\{\int k(t\alpha^{*}(x))f(x)\,dx\}^{-1}
so that
f_{t}(x)=c(t)k(t\alpha^{*}(x))f(x)
is our perturbed density function. Since
k(0)=k^{\prime}(0)=1,k^{\prime\prime}(0)=0
and
\max(\|k-1\|_{\infty},\|k^{\prime}\|_{\infty},\|k^{\prime\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}/8)\leq 1
we have for any
u\in\mathbb{R}
that

\displaystyle\bigl|k(u)-1\bigr|\leq\min\{1,|u|\}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad|k(u)-1-u|\leq 2|u|\min\{1,u^{2}\}.

(19)

In particular, since
\alpha^{*}\in L^{2}
and has mean zero, it follows from the dominated convergence theorem that

1/c(t)-1=\int k(t\alpha^{*}(x))f(x)\,dx-1=\int\{k(t\alpha^{*}(x))-1-t\alpha^{*}(x)\}f(x)\,dx=o(t^{2})

(20)

as
t\rightarrow 0
and hence that
c(t)=1+o(t^{2})
as
t\rightarrow 0
. Our next calculations will allow us to control the shifted marginal distributions of
f_{t}
on
\mathbb{R}^{S}
for
S\in\mathbb{S}
. Using (
19
), (
20
) and the assumption that
\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\|\bar{r}_{S}\|_{\infty}<\infty
we have that

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{\int f_{t}r_{S}}{\int fr_{S}}

\displaystyle-1-t\mathbb{E}\{(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\}\biggr|=\biggl|\int f(x)\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})\bigl\{c(t)k(t\alpha^{*}(x))-1-t\alpha^{*}(x)\bigr\}\,dx\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\biggl|\int f(x)\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})\bigl\{k(t\alpha^{*}(x))-1-t\alpha^{*}(x)\bigr\}\,dx\biggr|+o(t^{2})=o(t^{2})

(21)

as
t\rightarrow 0
. Using (
19
) and writing
x=(x_{S},x_{S^{c}})
and
(f_{t})_{S}
for the marginal density of
f
on
\mathbb{R}^{S}
, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
x_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
we have that

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{(f_{t})_{S}(x_{S})}{c(t)f_{S}(x_{S})}-1\biggr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\frac{\int k(t\alpha^{*}(x))f(x)\,dx_{S^{c}}}{f_{S}(x_{S})}-1\biggr|

\displaystyle=\frac{|\int\{k(t\alpha^{*}(x))-1\}f(x)\,dx_{S^{c}}|}{f_{S}(x_{S})}\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl[\min\{1,|t\alpha^{*}(X)|\}\bigm|X_{S}=x_{S}\bigr].

(22)

Similarly, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
x_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
we have that

\displaystyle\biggl|\frac{(f_{t})_{S}(x_{S})}{c(t)f_{S}(x_{S})}-1-t(\alpha^{*})_{S}(x_{S})\biggr|

\displaystyle=\frac{|\int\{k(t\alpha^{*}(x))-1-t\alpha^{*}(x)\}f(x)\,dx_{S^{c}}|}{f_{S}(x_{S})}

\displaystyle\leq 2|t|\mathbb{E}\bigl[|\alpha^{*}(X)|\min\{1,t^{2}\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}\bigm|X_{S}=x_{S}\bigr].

(23)

Equipped with the preceding bounds we will now establish the local asymptotic normality of our sequence of experiments. In the following calculation all arguments of logarithms are bounded away from
0
and
\infty
by the fact that
k
takes values in
[1/2,3/2]
. We can therefore see that for any fixed
t\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\displaystyle\biggl|\log\frac{dP_{n,t}}{dP_{n,0}}-\frac{t}{n^{1/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha^{*}(X_{i})-\frac{t}{n^{1/2}}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S}}\bigl[(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,i})-\mathbb{E}\{(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\}\bigr]+\frac{1}{2}\|t\|_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\biggr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\biggl\{\log\frac{f_{n^{-1/2}t}(X_{i})}{f(X_{i})}-\frac{t}{n^{1/2}}\alpha^{*}(X_{i})\biggr\}+\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}+\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathrm{Var}\bigl((\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S}}\biggl\{\log\biggl(\frac{(\int fr_{S})(f_{n^{-1/2}t})_{S}(X_{S,i})}{(\int f_{n^{-1/2}t}r_{S})f_{S}(X_{S,i})}\biggr)-\frac{t}{n^{1/2}}\bigl[(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,i})-\mathbb{E}\{(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\}\bigr]\biggr\}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}n_{S}\biggl|\log\frac{\int f_{n^{-1/2}t}r_{S}}{\int fr_{S}}-\frac{t}{n^{1/2}}\mathbb{E}\{(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\}+\frac{t^{2}\lambda_{S}}{2n_{S}}\mathbb{E}^{2}\{(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\biggl(n+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}n_{S}\biggr)\bigl|\log c(n^{-1/2}t)\bigr|+\frac{t^{2}}{2}\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha^{*}(X_{i})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\frac{t^{2}}{2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S}}(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,i})^{2}-\lambda_{S}\mathbb{E}\{(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})^{2}\}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\biggl|\log k(n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{*}(X_{i}))-\frac{t}{n^{1/2}}\alpha^{*}(X_{i})+\frac{t^{2}}{2n}\alpha^{*}(X_{i})^{2}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S}}\biggl|\log\biggl(\frac{(f_{n^{-1/2}t})_{S}(X_{S,i})}{c(n^{-1/2}t)f_{S}(X_{S,i})}\biggr)-\frac{t}{n^{1/2}}(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,i})+\frac{t^{2}}{2n}(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,i})^{2}\biggr|

\displaystyle\lesssim o_{p}(1)+\frac{t^{2}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha^{*}(X_{i})^{2}\min\bigl\{1,|n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{*}(X_{i})|\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.0pt+\frac{t^{2}}{n}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\min\{1,|n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{*}(X)|\}\bigm|X_{S}=X_{S,i}\bigr]

(24)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, under
P_{n,0}
, where the final bound follows from (
19
), (
20
), (
21
), (
22
) and (
23
). Now, since

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\min\bigl\{1,|n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{*}(X)|\bigr\}\bigr]=o(1)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
and since
\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\|\bar{r}_{S}\|_{\infty}<\infty
, we have by Markov’s inequality that the final two terms in (
24
) are
o_{p}(1)
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, under
P_{n,0}
. Writing
Z\sim N(0,1)
we therefore have that

\log\frac{dP_{n,t}}{dP_{n,0}}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\rightarrow}\|t\|_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}Z-\frac{1}{2}\|t\|_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, under
P_{n,0}
, for each
t\in\mathbb{R}
. This justifies our claim that our sequence of experiments is locally asymptotically normal.

Having establish local asymptotic normality, we now prove that the sequence of parameters
\theta(f_{n^{-1/2}t})
is regular. Indeed, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
we have by (
19
), (
20
) and the dominated convergence theorem that

\displaystyle\bigl|n^{1/2}

\displaystyle\{\theta(f_{n^{-1/2}t})-\theta(f)\}-t\mathbb{E}\{a(X)\alpha^{*}(X)\}\bigr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|n^{1/2}\int a(x)\{c(n^{-1/2}t)k(n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{*}(x))-1-n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{*}(x)\}f(x)\,dx\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq n^{1/2}\int|a(x)\{k(n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{*}(x))-1-n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{*}(x)\}|f(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+(3/2)n^{1/2}|c(n^{-1/2}t)-1|\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\{a(X)^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq 2|t|\int|a(x)\alpha^{*}(x)|\min\{1,t^{2}\alpha^{*}(x)^{2}/n\}f(x)\,dx+2n^{1/2}|c(n^{-1/2}t)-1|\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\{a(X)^{2}\}\rightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Using the notation of
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996, Chapter 3)
, we now see that our sequence of parameters is regular with norming operator
r_{n}=n^{1/2}
and
\dot{\kappa}(t)=t\mathbb{E}\{a(X)\alpha^{*}(X)\}
. We think of
\dot{\kappa}
as a linear map
\dot{\kappa}:\mathbb{R}_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{E}}
, where we write
\mathbb{R}_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}
for
\mathbb{R}
equipped with the inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}
and
\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{E}}
for
\mathbb{R}
equipped with the standard Euclidean inner product.

We must now find the adjoint operator
\dot{\kappa}^{*}:\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{E}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}
of
\dot{\kappa}
. For
S\in\mathbb{S}
and
\epsilon_{S}\in H_{S}
we will write, in a slight abuse of notation,
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}+\epsilon_{S}
for the element of
H_{\mathbb{S}}
that is equal to
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
except in the
S
th coordinate, where we replace
\alpha_{S}^{*}
by
\alpha_{S}^{*}+\epsilon_{S}
. Whenever
\eta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon_{S}\in H_{S}
satisfies
\int f_{S}\epsilon_{S}^{2}/\bar{r}_{S}<\infty
we have that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}+\eta\epsilon_{S})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\alpha^{*}(X)-\eta\epsilon_{S}(X_{S})\bigr)-\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\alpha^{*}(X)\bigr)+\int\frac{f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bigl(\alpha_{S}^{*}+\eta\epsilon_{S}\bigr)^{2}-\int\frac{f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\alpha_{S}^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle=-2\eta\,\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\alpha^{*}(X),\epsilon_{S}(X_{S})\bigr)+2\eta\int\frac{f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\alpha_{S}^{*}\epsilon_{S}+\eta^{2}\biggl\{\mathrm{Var}(\epsilon_{S}(X_{S}))+\int\frac{f_{S}\epsilon_{S}^{2}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=-2\eta\int f_{S}\epsilon_{S}\biggl\{(\alpha^{*})_{S}-\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\biggr\}+O(\eta^{2})

(25)

as
\eta\rightarrow 0
. After suitable centring, we may therefore conclude that

(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S})=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\alpha^{*}(X)\bigm|X_{S}\bigr\}=\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}-\int\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}

(26)

almost surely. Thus, for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\displaystyle\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}

\displaystyle=t_{1}t_{2}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathrm{Var}\bigl((\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle=t_{1}t_{2}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S,1})^{2}}{\lambda_{S}^{2}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})^{2}}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=t_{1}t_{2}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S,1})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S,1})\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=t_{1}t_{2}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S})\bigr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=t_{1}t_{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\alpha^{*}(X)\biggl\{\alpha^{*}(X)+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})\biggr\}\biggr]=t_{1}t_{2}\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)a(X)\}=t_{1}\cdot\dot{\kappa}(t_{2}),

(27)

and we see that the adjoint operator
\dot{\kappa}^{*}:\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{E}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}
is simply the identity map
\dot{\kappa}^{*}(t_{1})=t_{1}
.

Writing
\sigma^{2}=\|\dot{\kappa}^{*}1\|_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)a(X)\}
and letting
G\sim N(0,\sigma^{2})
, we may therefore apply Theorem 3.11.5 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996)
, (
26
) and the calculations in (
27
) to deduce that for any estimator sequence
(\theta_{n})
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{I}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\max_{t\in I}n\mathbb{E}_{f_{n^{-1/2}t}}\bigl[\bigl\{\theta_{n}-\theta(f_{n^{-1/2}t})\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)a(X)\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{S}}\int\frac{f_{S}}{\bar{r}_{S}}(\alpha_{S}^{*})^{2}=\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}),

where the supremum is taken over all finite subsets
I
of
\mathbb{R}
, as required.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9
.

By Proposition
6
we know that there exists a unique minimiser
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
and by (
25
) we know that it must satisfy the stationarity conditions (
26
). We prove our result by showing that
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
must take the form given in (
11
). Throughout this proof we use the shorthand
\alpha_{j}^{0}=\alpha_{[j]}^{*}
for
j\in[d-1]
such that
[j]\in\mathbb{S}
and
\alpha_{j}\equiv 0
otherwise and identify a function
g_{j}
of
y_{j}
with the random variable
g_{j}(Y_{j})
for notational convenience. We begin the proof by arguing inductively that there exist constants
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d-1}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\theta_{j}=0
if
[j]\not\in\mathbb{S}
and

\alpha_{j}^{0}=\frac{R_{j}}{1+R_{j}\mu_{j}}\biggl\{\mu_{j}(\tilde{a}_{j}-\alpha_{1}^{0}-\ldots-\alpha_{j-1}^{0})-\sum_{\ell=j}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\nu_{j}^{\ell-1}\biggr\}

(28)

for each
j\in[d-1]
. The arguments we give here are for the case that
\mathbb{S}=\{[1],\ldots,[d-1]\}
but they can be straightforwardly extended to cases where
\mathbb{S}\subset\{[1],\ldots,[d-1]\}
. We establish the base case
j=d-1
by noting that by (
26
) there must exist a constant
\theta_{d-1}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\theta_{d-1}+\frac{\alpha_{d-1}^{0}}{R_{d-1}}=\mathbb{E}\bigl(a-\theta-\alpha_{1}^{0}-\ldots-\alpha_{d-1}^{0}\bigm|Y_{d-1}\bigr)=\tilde{a}_{d-1}-\alpha_{1}^{0}-\ldots-\alpha_{d-1}^{0}.

It is now immediate that (
28
) holds with
j=d-1
on recalling that
\mu_{d-1}\equiv 1
and that
\nu_{d-1}^{d-2}\equiv 1
. Assuming that the induction hypothesis (
28
) holds at
j+1
and using the stationarity condition (
26
) and the tower law of expectation we see that there exists
\theta_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\theta_{j}+\frac{\alpha_{j}^{0}}{R_{j}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl(\frac{\alpha_{j+1}^{0}}{R_{j+1}}\biggm|Y_{j}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{1+R_{j+1}\mu_{j+1}}\biggl\{\mu_{j+1}(\tilde{a}_{j+1}-\alpha_{1}^{0}-\ldots-\alpha_{j}^{0})-\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\nu_{j+1}^{\ell-1}\biggr\}\biggm|Y_{j}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mu_{j}(\tilde{a}_{j}-\alpha_{1}^{0}-\ldots-\alpha_{j}^{0})-\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\nu_{j}^{\ell-1}

and it immediately follows that (
28
) holds at
j
.

We now use the fact that (
28
) holds for all
j\in[d-1]
to prove by induction that
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
satisfies (
11
). By (
28
) with
j=1
we know that

\alpha_{1}^{0}=\frac{R_{1}}{1/\mu_{1}+R_{1}}\biggl(\tilde{a}_{1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{1}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{1}}\biggr),

which coincides with (
11
) at
j=1
, establishing the base case. Using (
11
) as an induction hypothesis we have by (
28
) that

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}^{0}=\frac{R_{j}}{1+R_{j}\mu_{j}}\biggl\{\mu_{j}(\tilde{a}_{j}-\alpha_{1}^{0}-\ldots-\alpha_{j-1}^{0})-\sum_{\ell=j}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\nu_{j}^{\ell-1}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{R_{j}\mu_{j}}{1+R_{j}\mu_{j}}\biggl[\tilde{a}_{j}-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\frac{R_{i}\mu_{i}}{1+R_{i}\mu_{i}}\sum_{k=1}^{i}\biggl(\prod_{m=k}^{i-1}\frac{1}{1+R_{m}\mu_{m}}\biggr)\biggl\{\tilde{a}_{k}-\tilde{a}_{k-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\biggl(\frac{\nu_{k}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k}}-\frac{\nu_{k-1}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k-1}}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 395.0pt-\sum_{\ell=j}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{j}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{j}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{R_{j}\mu_{j}}{1+R_{j}\mu_{j}}\biggl[\tilde{a}_{j}-\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}\biggl\{\tilde{a}_{k}-\tilde{a}_{k-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\biggl(\frac{\nu_{k}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k}}-\frac{\nu_{k-1}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k-1}}\biggr)\biggr\}\sum_{i=k}^{j-1}\frac{R_{i}\mu_{i}}{1+R_{i}\mu_{i}}\biggl(\prod_{m=k}^{i-1}\frac{1}{1+R_{m}\mu_{m}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 395.0pt-\sum_{\ell=j}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{j}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{j}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{R_{j}\mu_{j}}{1+R_{j}\mu_{j}}\biggl[\tilde{a}_{j}-\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}\biggl\{\tilde{a}_{k}-\tilde{a}_{k-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\biggl(\frac{\nu_{k}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k}}-\frac{\nu_{k-1}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k-1}}\biggr)\biggr\}\biggl(1-\prod_{m=k}^{j-1}\frac{1}{1+R_{m}\mu_{m}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 395.0pt-\sum_{\ell=j}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{j}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{j}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{R_{j}\mu_{j}}{1+R_{j}\mu_{j}}\biggl[\tilde{a}_{j}-\tilde{a}_{j-1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{j-1}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{j-1}}-\sum_{\ell=j}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{j}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{j}}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt-\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}\biggl\{\tilde{a}_{k}-\tilde{a}_{k-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\biggl(\frac{\nu_{k}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k}}-\frac{\nu_{k-1}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k-1}}\biggr)\biggr\}\biggl(\prod_{m=k}^{j-1}\frac{1}{1+R_{m}\mu_{m}}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\frac{R_{j}\mu_{j}}{1+R_{j}\mu_{j}}\sum_{k=1}^{j}\biggl(\prod_{m=k}^{j-1}\frac{1}{1+R_{m}\mu_{m}}\biggr)\biggl\{\tilde{a}_{k}-\tilde{a}_{k-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\theta_{\ell}\biggl(\frac{\nu_{k}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k}}-\frac{\nu_{k-1}^{\ell-1}}{\mu_{k-1}}\biggr)\biggr\},

as required.
∎

Proof of Corollary
10
.

We begin by simplifying the
\mu_{j},\nu_{j}^{k}
and
\tilde{a}_{j}
in this MCAR setting. Since
R_{j}=\lambda_{[j]}
is constant for all
j\in[d-1]
and
\mu_{d-1}
is constant by definition, we can see that all the
\mu_{j}
are constants. For notational convenience, introduce
\lambda_{[j]}
for all
j\in[d-1]
by defining
\lambda_{[j]}=0
if
[j]\notin\mathbb{S}
. It is now straightforward to see inductively that

\mu_{j}=\frac{1}{1+\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}

for all
j\in[d-1]
. Similarly, we see that for any
k\geq j
we have

\nu_{j}^{k}=\prod_{m=j}^{k}\nu_{m}^{m}=\prod_{m=j}^{k}\frac{1+\sum_{\ell=m+2}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}{1+\sum_{\ell=m+1}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}=\frac{1+\sum_{\ell=k+2}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}{1+\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}

which, in particular, is constant. We also have that
\nu_{j}^{k}/\mu_{j}=1+\sum_{\ell=k+2}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}
does not depend on
j
except through the fact that
\nu_{j}^{k}/\mu_{j}=0
if
k\leq j-2
. Finally, we have

\tilde{a}_{j}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{j})=\mathbb{E}\{a(X)|X_{1},\ldots,X_{j}\}-\theta=a_{[j]}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{j})-\theta.

It now follows from (
11
) that the minimising choice of
\alpha_{[j]}
is of the form

\displaystyle\alpha_{[j]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{[j]}}{1+\sum_{\ell=j}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}\sum_{k=1}^{j}\biggl(\prod_{m=k}^{j-1}\frac{1+\sum_{\ell=m+1}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}{1+\sum_{\ell=m}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}\biggr)(a_{[k]}-a_{[k-1]}+\theta_{k-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{j}\frac{\lambda_{[j]}}{1+\sum_{\ell=k}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}(a_{[k]}-a_{[k-1]}+\theta_{k-1}).

We choose
\theta_{\ell}=0
for all
\ell\in[d-1]
in order that each
\alpha_{[j]}
has mean zero. This completes the proof.

In fact, we can also give a simple direct proof of the corollary, without using Proposition
9
. As in the proof of Proposition
7
, we check that the choice of
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}
given in the statement satisfies the stationarity conditions (
18
). Indeed, for
j\in[d-1]
such that
[j]\in\mathbb{S}
we have

\displaystyle(1+1/\lambda_{[j]})

\displaystyle\alpha_{[j]}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}\setminus\{[j]\}}\mathbb{E}(\alpha_{S}|X_{[j]})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{j}\frac{1}{1+\sum_{\ell=k}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}(a_{[k]}-a_{[k-1]})+\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{d-1}\sum_{k=1}^{j^{\prime}}\frac{\lambda_{[j^{\prime}]}}{1+\sum_{\ell=k}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}\mathbb{E}(a_{[k]}-a_{[k-1]}|X_{[j]})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{j}\frac{1}{1+\sum_{\ell=k}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}(a_{[k]}-a_{[k-1]})+\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{d-1}\sum_{k=1}^{j^{\prime}}\frac{\lambda_{[j^{\prime}]}}{1+\sum_{\ell=k}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}(a_{[k]}-a_{[k-1]})\mathbbm{1}_{\{k\leq j\}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{j}\frac{1}{1+\sum_{\ell=k}^{d-1}\lambda_{[\ell]}}\biggl(1+\sum_{j^{\prime}=k}^{d-1}\lambda_{[j^{\prime}]}\biggr)(a_{[k]}-a_{[k-1]})=\sum_{k=1}^{j}(a_{[k]}-a_{[k-1]})=a_{[j]}-\theta,

almost surely, as required.
∎

D.2
Proofs for Section
4

Proof of Proposition
12
.

In this proof it will be helpful to equip the set
H_{\mathbb{S}}=\prod_{S\in\mathbb{S}}H_{S}
with the alternative inner product

\langle\alpha_{\mathbb{S}},\beta_{\mathbb{S}}\rangle=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\alpha_{S}(X_{S})\beta_{S}(X_{S})\biggr\}.

where we write
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}=(\alpha_{S}:S\in\mathbb{S})
here and throughout the proof. For each
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}
we define
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})\in H_{\mathbb{S}}
by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}))_{S}(x_{S})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{S}(x_{S})-\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}\{a_{S}(x_{S})-\bar{\theta}_{S}^{(1)}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 65.0pt+\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\{\alpha_{S^{\prime}}(X_{S^{\prime}})|X_{S}=x_{S}\}-\frac{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\alpha_{S^{\prime}}f}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f}\biggr].

Note in particular that
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})=0
by the stationarity condition (
26
) and the fact that
\int\alpha_{S}^{*}f=0
. For
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}
we further define
Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})=\mathrm{Var}(\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}(X_{S}))+\sum_{S}\int\frac{\alpha_{S}^{2}f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}
. With these definitions, for any
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}},\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}
we may write

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})-\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})=-2\mathrm{Cov}\biggl(a(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}(X_{S}),\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\epsilon_{S}(X_{S})\biggr)+2\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{\alpha_{S}\epsilon_{S}f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}+Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\epsilon_{S}(X_{S})\biggl\{\frac{\alpha_{S}(X_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}-\mathbb{E}\biggl(a(X)-\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S^{\prime}}(X_{S^{\prime}})\biggm|X_{S}\biggr)\biggr\}\biggr]+Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}),\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\rangle+Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}),

(29)

which justifies our notation and thinking of
\nabla\mathcal{L}
as the gradient of
\mathcal{L}
. Comparing with the definition of
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}
in Section
4.1
, we see that we have the recurrence relation
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)}=\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-(\eta/2)\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})
, so that we may regard this iterative scheme as gradient descent.

To analyse the performance of this first-order optimisation algorithm we will use the smoothness and strong convexity of our problem. To this end, we now derive some useful properties of
\nabla\mathcal{L}
and
Q
. For any
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}},\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}
we have by (
29
) that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}),\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\bigl[\bigl\{\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}-\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})\bigr\}-Q(-\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-\bigl[\bigl\{\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})\bigr\}-Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})\bigr]

\displaystyle=2Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}).

(30)

From the definition of
\nabla\mathcal{L}
we also see, with a slight abuse of notation, that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bigl\{\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}})\bigr\}\bigr\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}\biggl\{\epsilon_{S}+\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\biggl(\mathbb{E}(\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}|X_{S})-\frac{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}f}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f}\biggr)\biggr\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int f\biggl[\frac{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\epsilon_{S}^{2}+2\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\epsilon_{S}\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}+\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\biggl\{\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\biggl(\mathbb{E}(\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}|X_{S})-\frac{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}f}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f}\biggr)\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int f\biggl[\frac{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\epsilon_{S}^{2}+2\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\epsilon_{S}\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}+\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\biggl\{\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\mathbb{E}(\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}|X_{S})\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int f\biggl\{\frac{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\epsilon_{S}^{2}+2\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\epsilon_{S}\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}+\biggl(\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl\{\int\frac{f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\epsilon_{S}^{2}+\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\sum_{S^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}}\epsilon_{S^{\prime}}\biggr)\biggr\}\leq|\mathbb{S}|Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}),

(31)

where the first inequality follows from the fact that variances are bounded by second moments and the second inequality follows from Jensen’s inequality. Using (
30
), (
31
) and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality we have for any
\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\neq 0
that

Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})=Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})^{-1}\frac{1}{4}\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}+\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}),\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\rangle^{2}\leq Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})^{-1}\{|\mathbb{S}|Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})\}\|\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\|^{2}=|\mathbb{S}|\|\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\|^{2},

(32)

and this clearly extends to the
\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}=0
case to show that our optimisation problem is
|\mathbb{S}|
-smooth.
We complement this with strong convexity by writing

Q(\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}})\geq\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{\epsilon_{S}^{2}f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\geq\frac{1}{1+C\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\int\frac{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}\epsilon_{S}^{2}=\frac{|\mathbb{S}|}{\kappa}\|\epsilon_{\mathbb{S}}\|^{2},

(33)

where we take condition number
\kappa=|\mathbb{S}|(1+C\lambda_{\mathrm{max}})
. Further, we can understand the convexity of the problem around the minimiser by writing

\displaystyle Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})\biggr)+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{(\alpha_{S}^{*})^{2}f_{S}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}=\mathrm{Var}\bigl(a(X)-\alpha^{*}(X)\bigr)-\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\alpha^{*}(X)\bigr)+\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\,a(X)-2\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)a(X)\bigr\}+\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})=\mathrm{Var}\,a(X)-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\leq\mathrm{Var}\,a(X),

(34)

where we use (
27
) for the final equality.

Having seen that our optimisation problem is smooth and strongly convex we are now in a position to conclude our proof. Choosing step size
\eta=|\mathbb{S}|^{-1}
and using (
43
), (
30
), (
31
), (
33
) and the fact that
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})=0
we have for any
M\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)}

\displaystyle-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}=\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}-\eta\langle\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 250.0pt+\frac{\eta^{2}}{4}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}-2\eta Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})+\frac{\eta^{2}}{4}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}-2\eta Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})+\eta^{2}|\mathbb{S}|Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}-|\mathbb{S}|^{-1}Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq(1-1/\kappa)\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\ldots\leq(1-1/\kappa)^{M+1}\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(0)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}=(1-1/\kappa)^{M+1}\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}.

It therefore follows from (
29
), (
31
), (
32
), (
33
) and (
34
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})=Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\leq|\mathbb{S}|\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}\leq|\mathbb{S}|(1-1/\kappa)^{M}\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa(1-1/\kappa)^{M}Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\leq\kappa(1-1/\kappa)^{M}\mathrm{Var}\,a(X),

as required.

The final claim in our statement is a simple consequence of (
29
) and (
32
). Indeed, we may write

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})=\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}),\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}\rangle+Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})

\displaystyle=-(2/\eta)\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}\|^{2}+Q(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})\leq(-2/\eta+|\mathbb{S}|)\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=-|\mathbb{S}|\|\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M+1)}-\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}\|^{2}\leq 0,

as claimed.

∎

Proof of Proposition
13
.

We start by proving the first statement. For
S\in\mathbb{S}
and
\ell=1,2
define

\displaystyle R_{S,\ell}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{3-\ell}}\{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(x)

\displaystyle-\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(\ell)}^{(M)}(x)\}

\displaystyle-\frac{|\mathcal{D}_{3-\ell}|}{n\cdot n_{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}\biggl[\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{S,j})-\frac{\widehat{r}_{S,(3-\ell)}}{\mathbb{E}\{r_{S}(X_{S})\}}\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(\ell)}^{(M)}(X_{S,j})\biggr],

so that we may write
\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)}=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}(R_{S,1}+R_{S,2})
. Our goal now is to show that each of these remainder terms is negligible, by bounding
\mathbb{E}(R_{S,\ell}^{2})
for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
and
\ell=1,2
. The
\ell=2
terms are bounded very similarly to the
\ell=1
terms, so we focus on the latter for notational ease. We start with the simple bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{\widehat{r}_{S,(2)}}{\mathbb{E}\{r_{S}(X_{S})\}}-1\biggr)^{2}\biggr]=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{2}}\bar{r}_{S}(x)-1\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}\mathrm{Var}\,\bar{r}_{S}(X)\leq\frac{C}{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}.

(35)

Now, by conditioning on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and using the fact that
\int\alpha_{S}^{(M)}f_{S}=0
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{1}{n_{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{S,j})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl(\int\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}f_{S}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{1}{n_{S}}\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\frac{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{S,1})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{1}\biggr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\int(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})f_{S}\biggr\}^{2}+\frac{1}{n_{S}}\int\frac{(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)})^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int\biggl(1+\frac{2}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\biggr)(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\frac{2}{n_{S}}\int\frac{(\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}.

(36)

The calculations in (
35
) and (
36
) will allow us to control the error arising from using the estimator
\widehat{r}_{S,(2)}
in the second term of
R_{S,1}
. The other portion of the error is bounded as follows. Conditioning on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{2}}\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(x_{S})-\bar{\alpha}_{S}^{(M)}(x_{S})\}-\frac{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}{n\cdot n_{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\frac{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{S,j})-\bar{\alpha}_{S}^{(M)}(X_{S,j})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}{n^{2}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X)-\bar{\alpha}_{S}^{(M)}(X)\bigm|\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)+\frac{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|^{2}}{n^{2}n_{S}}\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\frac{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{S,1})-\bar{\alpha}_{S}^{(M)}(X_{S,1})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{1}\biggr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int\frac{n+n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\},

(37)

where the first equality relies on the fact that the quantity in braces has mean zero conditionally on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Combining (
35
), (
36
) and (
37
) we now have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(R_{S,1}^{2})\leq\frac{2|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int\frac{n+n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}

\displaystyle+2\Bigl(\frac{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}{n}\Bigr)^{2}\frac{C}{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int\Bigl(1+\frac{2}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\Bigr)(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\frac{2}{n_{S}}\int\frac{(\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2|\mathcal{D}_{2}|(C+1)}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int\frac{n+n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\frac{4|\mathcal{D}_{2}|C}{n^{2}n_{S}}\int\frac{(\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}.

It follows from the fact that
\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)}=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}(R_{S,1}+R_{S,2})
and the line above that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathbb{S}|\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\{\mathbb{E}(R_{S,1}^{2})+\mathbb{E}(R_{S,2}^{2})\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8C|\mathbb{S}|}{n}\max_{\ell=1,2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl[\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int\frac{n+n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(\ell)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\frac{1}{n_{S}}\int\frac{(\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8C|\mathbb{S}|}{n}\biggl[\max_{\ell=1,2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int\frac{n+n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(\ell)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})\biggr],

as required.

We finally turn to the second statement of the proposition, where we give a bound on
\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})
. Recall that we have
\mathbb{E}\{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{S,1})/\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})\}=\mathbb{E}\{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)\}=0
for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
. It therefore follows that
\theta^{*,(M)}
is unbiased and that

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})=\mathrm{Var}\biggl(a(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{S})\biggr)+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{n}{n_{S}}\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{S,1})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl(\frac{n}{n_{S}}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{S}}\biggr)\int\frac{f_{S}}{\bar{r}_{S}}(\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}\leq\biggl(1+\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl|\frac{n\lambda_{S}}{n_{S}}-1\biggr|\biggr)\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}),

as claimed.

∎

The proof of Proposition
14
to come relies on the following result.

Lemma 24
.

Let
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}\in(0,1]
and
L_{1},L_{2},L_{3}\in(0,\infty)
, and suppose that
a
, the distribution of
X
and
(r_{S}:S\in\mathbb{S})
satisfy (A1)(
\beta_{1},L_{1}
), (A2)(
\beta_{2},L_{2}
) and (A3)(
\beta_{3},L_{3}
), respectively. Then for any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
and
x_{S_{m}},x_{S_{m}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{S_{m}}
with
\|x_{S_{m}}-x_{S_{m}}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
we haves

|\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S_{m}})-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S_{m}}^{\prime})|\leq\bigl\{L_{1}+2(2^{m}-1)\bigl(L_{2}+L_{3}\bigr)\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}\|x_{S_{m}}-x_{S_{m}}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{\wedge}},

where we write
\beta_{\wedge}=\beta_{1}\wedge\beta_{2}\wedge\beta_{3}
.

Proof of Lemma
24
.

We begin be establishing simple uniform bounds on the functions
\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}
. For any
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
and
S\in\mathbb{S}
such that
S\neq S_{m}
and any
x_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
we have that

|\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})|=\biggl|\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}|X_{S}=x_{S}\}\biggr|\leq 2\|\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}\|_{\infty}\leq\ldots\leq 2^{m+1}\|a\|_{\infty}.

(38)

We now prove the main claim inductively, noting that the
m=0
base case is a simple consequence of (A1)(
\beta_{1},L_{1}
). Now suppose that the claim holds at a given
m
and let
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
and
S\in\mathbb{S}
be such that
S\neq S_{m}
. For any
x_{S},x_{S}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}
with
\|x_{S}-x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle|\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})

\displaystyle-\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}|X_{S}=x_{S}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt-\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}|X_{S}=x_{S}^{\prime}\}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl|\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)|X_{S}=x_{S}\}-\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)|X_{S}=x_{S}^{\prime}\}\bigr|

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+2\|\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}\|_{\infty}\biggl|\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}-\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})}\biggr|.

(39)

Using (A2)(
\beta_{2},L_{2}
), our induction hypothesis and (
38
) the first term in (
39
) can be controlled by writing

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}

\displaystyle(x_{S},X_{S^{c}})|X_{S}=x_{S}\}-\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S}^{\prime},X_{S^{c}})|X_{S}=x_{S}^{\prime}\}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl|\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S},X_{S^{c}})|X_{S}=x_{S}\}-\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S},X_{S^{c}})|X_{S}=x_{S}^{\prime}\}\bigr|

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+\bigl|\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S},X_{S^{c}})-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S}^{\prime},X_{S^{c}})|X_{S}=x_{S}^{\prime}\bigr\}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{2}\|\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}\|_{\infty}\|x_{S}-x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{2}}+\bigl\{L_{1}+2(2^{m}-1)\bigl(L_{2}+L_{3}\bigr)\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}\|x_{S}-x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{\wedge}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{m+1}L_{2}\|a\|_{\infty}\|x_{S}-x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{2}}+\bigl\{L_{1}+2(2^{m}-1)\bigl(L_{2}+L_{3}\bigr)\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}\|x_{S}-x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{\wedge}}.

(40)

Moreover, using (A3)(
\beta_{3},L_{3}
) the second term in (
39
) can be written as

\displaystyle 2\|\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}\|_{\infty}\biggl|\frac{1}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}-\frac{1}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{m+1}\|a\|_{\infty}\frac{\lambda_{S}|\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})-\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})|}{\{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})\}\{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})\}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{m+1}\|a\|_{\infty}L_{3}\|x_{S}-x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{3}}.

(41)

Combining (
39
), (
40
) and (
41
) we see that

\displaystyle|\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})

\displaystyle-\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\{L_{1}+\{2(2^{m}-1)+2^{m+1}\}\bigl(L_{2}+L_{3}\bigr)\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}\|x_{S}-x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{\wedge}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{L_{1}+2(2^{m+1}-1)\bigl(L_{2}+L_{3}\bigr)\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}\|x_{S}-x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{\wedge}}.

as required.
∎

Proof of Proposition
14
.

We begin by expressing
\alpha_{S}^{(M)}
and
\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}
in terms of simpler pieces. To this end, given
m\in\mathbb{N}
write
\mathbb{S}^{(m)}=\{(S_{1},\ldots,S_{m})\in\mathbb{S}^{m}:S_{j+1}\neq S_{j}\text{ for }j\in[m-1]\}
and recursively define functions
\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(\cdot)
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
as follows. We initialise by setting
\bar{a}_{S}^{(1)}(x_{S})
to be equal to the right-hand side of (
10
). For
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{S}=(S_{1},\ldots,S_{m})\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
and
S\in\mathbb{S}\setminus\{S_{m}\}
we then set

\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x)=\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}|X_{S}=x_{S}\},\quad\text{where}\quad\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}=\frac{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}f}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}}.

For
M\in\mathbb{N}_{0},m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\eta\in[0,1]
let
B\sim\mathrm{Bin}(M,\eta)
to introduce the notation
b_{M,\eta}(m)=\mathbb{P}(B\geq m)
, where we say that
B=0
almost surely if
M=0
. We claim that, for
S\in\mathbb{S}
,
M\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\eta\in(0,1]
we may write

\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(x)=\sum_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}:S_{m}=S}b_{M,\eta}(m)\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x),

(42)

where we note that
\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(x)
is a function of
x_{S}
only. The
M=0
case is trivial and we proceed by induction on
M
. Assuming the induction hypothesis, for any
M\geq 0
we have that

\displaystyle\alpha_{S}^{(M+1)}=\alpha_{S}^{(M)}-(\eta/2)\{\nabla\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})\}_{S}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{S}^{(M)}-\eta\biggl[\alpha_{S}^{(M)}-\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(a_{S}-\bar{\theta}_{S}^{(1)})+\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\biggl\{\mathbb{E}(\alpha_{S^{\prime}}^{(M)}|X_{S})-\frac{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\alpha_{S^{\prime}}^{(M)}f}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)\sum_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}:S_{m}=S}b_{M,\eta}(m)\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+\eta\bar{a}_{S}^{(1)}-\eta\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\sum_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}:S_{m}=S^{\prime}}b_{M,\eta}(m)\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M+1}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}:S_{m}=S}\bigl\{(1-\eta)b_{M,\eta}(m)+\eta b_{M,\eta}(m-1)\bigr\}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{M+1}(-1)^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}:S_{m}=S}b_{M+1,\eta}(m)\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)},

(43)

as required.

Having decomposed
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)}
, we now aim for a similar decomposition of
\widehat{\alpha}_{\mathbb{S},(1)}^{(M)}
. Initialise by setting
\widehat{a}^{(0)}\equiv a
and, for
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
and
(\boldsymbol{\ell},\ell)=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m},\ell)
with
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}<\ell\leq M
, iteratively define

\displaystyle\widehat{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell},\ell)}(x_{S})

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(x_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(\ell)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(x_{S})}\biggl\{|\mathcal{D}_{1,\ell}|^{-1}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,\ell}}\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(\ell)}(x_{S})}

\displaystyle\hskip 190.0pt-\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,\ell}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(\ell)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,\ell}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(\ell)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}\biggr\},

when
\|x_{S}\|_{\infty}\leq T
and
\widehat{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})=0
otherwise. We claim that we may write

\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M_{0})}(x_{S})=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{0}}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}\\
S_{m}=S\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}\leq M_{0}\end{subarray}}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{M_{0}-m-\ell_{1}+1}\,\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}(x_{S})

for any
M_{0}=0,1,\ldots,M
. Trivially, the base case
M_{0}=0
holds as both sides of the equality are zero. Proceeding by induction, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M_{0}+1)}(x_{S})=(1-\eta)\widehat{\alpha}_{S}^{(M_{0})}(x_{S})

\displaystyle+\eta\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(x_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(M_{0}+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(x_{S})}\biggl[|\mathcal{D}_{1,M_{0}+1}|^{-1}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,M_{0}+1}}\biggl\{a(y)-\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M_{0})}(y)\biggr\}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(M_{0}+1)}(x_{S})}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,M_{0}+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(M_{0}+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}\{a(y)-\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M_{0})}(y)\}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,M_{0}+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(M_{0}+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)\sum_{m=1}^{M_{0}}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}\\
S_{m}=S\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}\leq M_{0}\end{subarray}}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{M_{0}-m-\ell_{1}+1}\,\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}(x_{S})+\eta\widehat{a}_{S}^{(M_{0}+1)}(x_{S})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-\eta\sum_{S^{\prime}\neq S}\sum_{m=1}^{M_{0}}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}\\
S_{m}=S^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}\leq M_{0}\end{subarray}}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{M_{0}-m-\ell_{1}+1}\,\widehat{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell},M_{0}+1)}(x_{S})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{0}}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}\\
S_{m}=S\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}\leq M_{0}\end{subarray}}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{M_{0}-m-\ell_{1}+2}\,\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}(x_{S})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\sum_{m=1}^{M_{0}+1}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}\\
S_{m}=S\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}=M_{0}+1\end{subarray}}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{M_{0}-m-\ell_{1}+2}\,\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}(x_{S})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{0}+1}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}\\
S_{m}=S\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}\leq M_{0}+1\end{subarray}}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{(M_{0}+1)-m-\ell_{1}+1}\,\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}(x_{S}),

as required.

We now reduce the error of
\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}
to the error of its constituent parts in the ball of radius
T
about the origin. This requires an understanding of the weights in the previous decompositions. First, for a fixed
m\in\mathbb{N}
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}\leq M\end{subarray}}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{M-m-\ell_{1}+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{M-m+1}\binom{M-\ell_{1}}{m-1}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{M-m-\ell_{1}+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=m-1}^{M-1}\binom{\ell}{m-1}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{\ell-m+1}=b_{M,\eta}(m),

where the final identity can be checked by induction on
M
. Now, recalling that we write
B\sim\mathrm{Bin}(M,\eta)
with
\eta=|\mathbb{S}|^{-1}
, in the case that
|\mathbb{S}|>1
we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}:S_{m}=S}b_{M,\eta}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}|\mathbb{S}|^{m-1}b_{M,\eta}(m)=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\biggl(\sum_{m=1}^{j}|\mathbb{S}|^{m-1}\biggr)\mathbb{P}(B=j)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\frac{|\mathbb{S}|^{j}-1}{|\mathbb{S}|-1}\mathbb{P}(B=j)=\frac{\{(1-\eta)+\eta|\mathbb{S}|\}^{M}}{|\mathbb{S}|-1}\leq 2^{M},

(44)

and it is straightforward to see that this bound continues to hold when
|\mathbb{S}|=1
. Now we have by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int\biggl[\sum_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{m-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}\\
S_{m}=S\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}=(\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<\ldots<\ell_{m}\leq M\end{subarray}}\eta^{m}(1-\eta)^{(M_{0}+1)-m-\ell_{1}+1}\bigl(\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}\bigr)\biggr]^{2}f_{S}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2M}\max_{m=1,\ldots,M}\max_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}:S_{m}=S}\mathbb{E}\int\bigl(\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(1,\ldots,m)}-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}\bigr)^{2}f_{S},

(45)

where the final inequality we use the fact that
\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell})}
has the same distribution for any
\boldsymbol{\ell}
in the range of the sum. For notational simplicity, in the remainder of the proof we write
\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}
for
\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(1,\ldots,m)}
For
S\in\mathbb{S}
write
B_{T}^{S}=\{x_{S}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{S}:\|x_{S}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq T\}
. The contribution to the error from outside this ball can be bounded using

\int_{(B_{T}^{S})^{c}}\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S})-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S})\bigr\}^{2}f_{S}(x_{S})\,dx_{S}\leq 2^{2m}\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T),

(46)

which follows from the fact that
\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S})=0
when
x_{S}\not\in B_{T}^{S}
and (
38
).

It now suffices to bound the error of the individual estimators
\widehat{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})
when
\|x_{S}\|_{\infty}\leq T
and
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m-1)}
with
S_{m}\neq S
.
Using the fact that
\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(x_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(x_{S})}\leq 1
and the triangle inequality we have that

\displaystyle\bigl|\widehat{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})-\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl[|\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)|X_{S}=x_{S})\}|+|\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}|\bigr]\biggl|\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(x_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(x_{S})}-\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\biggl||\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{-1}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\biggl||\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{-1}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})}-\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)|X_{S}=x_{S}\}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\biggl|\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\biggl|\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}-\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\biggl|\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}-\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}}\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\biggl|\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}}-\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}\biggr|

\displaystyle=:\sum_{j=1}^{7}R_{(\mathbf{S},S),j}^{(m+1)}(x_{S}).

(47)

We now bound each of these error terms separately.

To bound
R_{1}
:

Using the fact that
|a/(1+\epsilon+a)-a/(1+a)|\leq|\epsilon|
for any
a\geq 0
and
\epsilon\geq-1
, and using very similar arguments to (
35
) and (
38
) above, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{R_{(\mathbf{S},S),1}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl[|\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)|X_{S}=x_{S})\}|+|\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}|\bigr]^{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\biggl[\frac{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}}{\mathbb{E}\{r_{S}(X_{S})\}}-1\biggr]^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2m+2}C\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}.

(48)

To bound
R_{2}
:
We use the notation
f_{S,h}(x_{S})=(K_{h}^{S}\ast f_{S})(x_{S})
. By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and, using the fact that
K
is uniform, Lemma 4.1(i) of
Györfi et al. (2002)
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{

\displaystyle R_{(\mathbf{S},S),2}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\}^{2}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\sum_{y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S}^{\prime})}\biggm|y\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\}^{2}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\bigr\}^{2}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-X_{S})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\bigr\}^{2}+\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}^{c}}(X)\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)^{2}\biggr\}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-X_{S})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\bigr\}^{2}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-X_{S})\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 175.0pt+\frac{2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}^{c}}(X)K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-X_{S})\bigr\},

(49)

where the final inequality uses (
38
).

To bound
R_{3}
:
Our bounds on this term are based on ideas from the theory of nonparametric regression with kernels, for which a good reference is Chapter 5 of
Györfi et al. (2002)
. Writing
\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})=\mathbb{E}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)|X_{S}=x_{S}\}
, we may decompose this error term by writing

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{R_{(\mathbf{S},S),3}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{-1}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})}-\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})\biggr\}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})>0\}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})=0)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{-1}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(y_{S})\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{-1}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(y_{S})-\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})=0)

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{-2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(y_{S})\}^{2}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})^{2}}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{-1}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(y_{S})-\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})=0).

(50)

We proceed by bounding these three terms separately. Since
K
is the uniform kernel, we may appeal to Lemma 4.1(ii) of
Györfi et al. (2002)
and (
38
) to see that the first term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{(2h)^{-|S|}K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-y_{S})}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}}\biggr]=\frac{2^{2m-|S|}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{h^{-|S|}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})>0\}}}{\widehat{f}_{S}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|h^{|S|}f_{S,h}(x_{S})}.

(51)

Using (
38
), the third term on the right-hand side of (
50
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle 2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\bigl\{1-(2h)^{|S|}f_{S,h}(x_{S})\bigr\}^{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\exp\bigl(-|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|(2h)^{|S|}f_{S,h}(x_{S})\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|(2h)^{|S|}f_{S,h}(x_{S})}.

(52)

We bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
50
) using the smoothness properties of
\tilde{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}
. It follows from Lemma
24
that this term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\bigl\{L_{1}+2(2^{m}-1)(L_{2}+L_{3})\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}^{2}h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}.

(53)

It now follows from (
50
), (
51
), (
52
) and (
53
) that

\mathbb{E}\{R_{(\mathbf{S},S),3}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}\leq\frac{3\times 2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|h^{|S|}f_{S,h}(x_{S})}+\bigl\{L_{1}+2(2^{m}-1)(L_{2}+L_{3})\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}^{2}h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}.

(54)

To bound
R_{4}
:
This term, as well as those that follow, do not vary with
x_{S}
. We may use Cauchy–Schwarz to write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{R_{(\mathbf{S},S),4}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{c}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)\bigr],

(55)

where the final inequality follows from the assumption that
c\leq r_{S}(y)\leq C
for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

To bound
R_{5}
:
Using the fact that
|a/(1+\epsilon+a)-a/(1+a)|\leq|\epsilon|a/(1+a+\epsilon)
whenever
a\geq 0
and
\epsilon\geq-1
, we may write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{R_{(\mathbf{S},S),5}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\{\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}-\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}}{\mathbb{E}\{r_{S}(X_{S})\}}-1\biggr\}^{2}\biggl\{\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]
