\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{c}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{4}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\bigl|\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\bigr|\biggr\}^{2}\biggl\{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\bar{r}_{S}(y)-|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{c}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{4}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sum_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}|\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y_{1})||\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y_{2})|\{\bar{r}_{S}(y_{3})-1\}\{\bar{r}_{S}(y_{4})-1\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{2}}{c}\frac{3}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2},

(56)

where the final inequality follows from (
38
) and the fact that terms in the sum where
y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}
are distinct have mean zero.

To bound
R_{6}
:
This term can be bounded similarly to the one above. Indeed, again using the fact that
|a/(1+\epsilon+a)-a/(1+a)|\leq|\epsilon|a/(1+a+\epsilon)
whenever
a\geq 0
and
\epsilon\geq-1
, we may write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{R_{(\mathbf{S},S),6}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{(\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)})(\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)})}\biggr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt\times\biggl\{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}+\lambda_{S}r_{S}(y)}-\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}}\biggr\}^{2}\biggl\{\frac{\widehat{r}_{S}^{(m+1)}}{\mathbb{E}\{r_{S}(X_{S})\}}-1\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{c}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|^{4}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\bigl|\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x)\bigr|\biggr\}^{2}\biggl\{\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\bar{r}_{S}(x)-|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{2}}{c}\frac{3}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2},

(57)

where the final inequality uses from (
56
) above.

To bound
R_{7}
:
We first deal with the error incurred by truncating to
B_{T}
. We have

\displaystyle\biggl(\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}-\frac{\int_{B_{T}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}f}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f}\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\frac{\int_{B_{T}^{c}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}f_{S}}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}}\biggr)^{2}\leq\frac{\int_{B_{T}^{c}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)})^{2}f_{S}}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{c}\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T).

(58)

The rest of this remainder term can be bounded by writing

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{R_{(\mathbf{S},S),7}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}-2\biggl(\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}-\frac{\int_{B_{T}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}f_{S}}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}}\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\biggl|\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}}-\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}\biggr|^{2}\biggr\}-2\biggl(\bar{\theta}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}-\frac{\int_{B_{T}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}f_{S}}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}}\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\biggl|\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)}{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}}-\frac{\int_{B_{T}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}f_{S}}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}}\biggr|^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\sum_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(y)}\Bigl(\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(y)-\frac{\int_{B_{T}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}f_{S}}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}}\Bigr)}{\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x)}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x)}}\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{(\frac{\lambda_{S}c}{1+\lambda_{S}c})^{2}}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl(\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggl\{\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)-\frac{\int_{B_{T}}\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}f_{S}}{\int\frac{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{1+\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}f_{S}}\biggr\}\biggr)^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2m+1}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}\biggl(\frac{C}{c}\biggr)^{2}.

(59)

It is an immediate consequence of (
58
) and (
59
) that

\mathbb{E}\{R_{(\mathbf{S},S),7}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})^{2}\}\leq\frac{2^{2m+1}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}\biggl(\frac{C}{c}\biggr)^{2}+2^{2m+1}\frac{C\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{c}\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T).

(60)

Having bounded all of the remainder terms we now combine our calculations. It follows from (
47
), (
48
), (
49
), (
54
), (
55
), (
56
), (
57
) and (
60
) that for any
x_{S}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})-\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 7\biggl[\frac{2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|}\biggl\{12\biggl(\frac{C}{c}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{3}{h^{|S|}f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\biggr\}+\bigl\{L_{1}+2(2^{m}-1)(L_{2}+L_{3})\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}^{2}h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+2^{2m+1}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\frac{C}{c}\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)+\frac{2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}^{c}}(X)K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-X_{S})\bigr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+7\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)\biggl\{\frac{C}{c}+\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x_{S}-X_{S})}{f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\biggr\}\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\bigr\}^{2}\biggr].

(61)

We now aim to integrate this bound over
x_{S}\in B_{T}^{S}
. This requires two preliminary calculations, both of which use similar arguments to those leading up to (5.1) in the proof of Theorem 5.1 of
Györfi et al. (2002)
. To this end, writing
B_{z}(r)=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{S}:\|x-z\|_{\infty}\leq r\}
, let
z_{1},\ldots,z_{N}\in B_{T}^{S}
be such that
B_{T}^{S}\subseteq\cup_{j=1}^{N}B_{z_{j}}(h/2)
and such that
N\leq(4T/h)^{|S|}
. First, recalling that
f_{S,h}(x_{S})=(2h)^{-|S|}\int_{B_{x_{S}}(h)}f_{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)}{f_{S,h}(X_{S})}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{B_{z_{j}}(h/2)}\frac{f_{S}(x_{S})}{f_{S,h}(x_{S})}\,dx_{S}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N}\int_{B_{z_{j}}(h/2)}\frac{f_{S}(x_{S})}{(2h)^{-|S|}\int_{B_{z_{j}}(h/2)}f_{S}}\,dx_{S}=(2h)^{|S|}N\leq(8T)^{|S|}.

(62)

Similarly, we can partition
B_{x_{S}}(h)
into
2^{|S|}
balls of radius
h/2
to see that

\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(X_{S}-x_{S})}{f_{S,h}(X_{S})}\biggr\}=\int_{B_{x_{S}}(h)}\frac{f_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})}{\int_{B_{x_{S}^{\prime}}(h)}f_{S}}\,dx_{S}^{\prime}\leq 2^{|S|}.

(63)

We recall that we write
\widehat{a}^{(0)}\equiv a
and note that the arguments leading to (
61
) go through for the
m=0
case by taking
\bar{a}^{(0)}\equiv a
. Using the bound
|\mathcal{D}_{1,m+1}|\geq n/(4M)
it now follows from (
61
), (
62
) (
63
) and inductive reasoning that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(X)-\bar{a}_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(X)\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 7\biggl[\frac{2^{2m+2}M\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{n}\biggl\{12\biggl(\frac{C}{c}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{3(8T)^{d}}{h^{d}}\biggr\}+\bigl\{L_{1}+2(2^{m}-1)(L_{2}+L_{3})\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}^{2}h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+2^{2m+1}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{C}{c}+2^{d-1}\biggr)\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+7\biggl(\frac{C}{c}+2^{d}\biggr)\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{B_{T}}(X)\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq 7\sum_{j=0}^{m}\biggl\{7\biggl(\frac{C}{c}+2^{d}\biggr)\biggr\}^{m-j}\biggl[\frac{2^{2j+2}M\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{n}\biggl\{12\biggl(\frac{C}{c}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{3(8T)^{d}}{h^{d}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\bigl\{L_{1}+2^{j+1}(L_{2}+L_{3})\|a\|_{\infty}\bigr\}^{2}h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+2^{2j+1}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{C}{c}+2^{d-1}\biggr)\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 14\max\bigl(\|a\|_{\infty}^{2},1\bigr)\biggl[\frac{2M}{n}\biggl\{12\biggl(\frac{C}{c}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{3(8T)^{d}}{h^{d}}\biggr\}+2(L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3})^{2}h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\biggl(\frac{C}{c}+2^{d-1}\biggr)\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr]\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j}\biggl\{7\biggl(\frac{C}{c}+2^{d}\biggr)\biggr\}^{m-j}

\displaystyle\leq 14\biggl\{7\biggl(\frac{C}{c}+2^{d}\biggr)\biggr\}^{m+1}\max\bigl(\|a\|_{\infty}^{2},1\bigr)\max\{(C/c)^{2},(L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3})^{2},8^{d}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt\times\biggl[\frac{M}{n}\Bigl(\frac{T}{h}\Bigr)^{d}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr].

Substituting this bound and (
46
) into (
45
) and leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\bar{\alpha}_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2M+4}7^{M}\biggl(\frac{C}{c}+2^{d}\biggr)^{M}\max\bigl(\|a\|_{\infty}^{2},1\bigr)\max\{(C/c)^{2},(L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3})^{2},8^{d}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt\times\biggl[\frac{M}{n}\Bigl(\frac{T}{h}\Bigr)^{d}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr]

and the result follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
15
.

We begin by writing

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\}-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle=n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})+2n\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})(\theta^{*,(M)}-\theta)\bigr\}+n\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})+2n\mathrm{Var}^{1/2}(\theta^{*,(M)})\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigl\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\bigr\}+n\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{L}^{1/2}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigl\{n(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\bigr\}+\biggl[\bigl\{n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\bigr\}^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigl\{n(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\bigr\}\biggr]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathrm{Var}^{1/2}\{a(X)\}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigl\{n(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\bigr\}+2\mathbb{E}\bigl\{n(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\bigr\}+2\bigl\{n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\bigr\}.

Now the first two terms can be bounded using Propositions
12
,
13
and
14
while the final term is bounded immediately by Propositions
12
and
13
. Indeed, using the shorthand
n_{\min}=\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}}n_{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigl\{n(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\bigr\}\leq 8C|\mathbb{S}|\biggl[\max_{\ell=1,2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int\frac{n+n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}}(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(\ell)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8C|\mathbb{S}|^{2}(n+cn_{\min})}{cn_{\min}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell=1,2\\
S\in\mathbb{S}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(\ell)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\frac{8C|\mathbb{S}|}{n}\biggl(1+\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl|\frac{n\lambda_{S}}{n_{S}}-1\biggr|\biggr)\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8C|\mathbb{S}|^{2}(n+cn_{\min})}{cn_{\min}}A^{M}B\biggl\{\frac{M}{n}\Bigl(\frac{T}{h}\Bigr)^{d}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}+\frac{16C|\mathbb{S}|\mathrm{Var}\{a(X)\}}{n}

(64)

where the first and second inequalities follow from Proposition
13
and the third inequality follows from Propositions
12
and
14
and our technical assumption that
|n\lambda_{S}/n_{S}-1|\leq 1/n
. It is straightforward from Propositions
12
and
13
that

\displaystyle n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq(1+1/n)\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq(1+1/n)\bigl\{\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})+\kappa(1-1/\kappa)^{M}\mathrm{Var}\,a(X)\bigr\}-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\{2\kappa(1-1/\kappa)^{M}+1/n\bigr\}\mathrm{Var}\,a(X).

Recalling the values of
A
and
B
from the statement of Proposition
14
, straightforward but tedious calculations show that

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\}-\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq 200|\mathbb{S}|^{2}(C/c)(1+\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1})A^{M}B\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/2}\!\!\!\!\!+4\kappa\exp(-M/\kappa)\mathrm{Var}\,a(X)

and the result follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
16
.

The first component of the proof is in establishing the consistency of the variance estimator
\widehat{V}
. It is sufficient to prove the consistency of the individual estimators
\widehat{V}^{(\ell)}
and
\widehat{V}_{S}^{(\ell)}
for
S\in\mathbb{S}
and
\ell=1,2
. It will be convenient to introduce the oracle estimators

V^{*,(\ell)}=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{3-\ell}|}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{3-\ell}}\biggl\{a(x)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(x)\biggr\}^{2}-\theta^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad V_{S}^{*,(\ell)}=\frac{n}{|\mathcal{D}_{3-\ell}|}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{3-\ell}}\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(x)^{2}}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x)},

to which we will compare our data-driven estimators. By symmetry we may restrict attention to the
\ell=1
estimators. First, we see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|

\displaystyle\widehat{V}^{(1)}-V^{*,(1)}|\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl|\biggl\{a(X_{n})-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})\biggr\}^{2}-\biggl\{a(X_{n})-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\biggr\}^{2}+\theta^{2}-(\widehat{\theta})^{2}\biggr|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl|\biggl\{2a(X_{n})-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\bigl(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})+\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr)\biggr\}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 325.0pt+(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)(\widehat{\theta}+\theta)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[2\biggl|\biggl\{a(X_{n})-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\biggr\}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\}\biggr|+2\theta|\widehat{\theta}-\theta|

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+\biggl\{\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\}\biggr\}^{2}+(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathcal{L}^{1/2}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}[\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\}^{2}]+2|\theta|\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\theta}-\theta|

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+|\mathbb{S}|\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}[\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\},

(65)

where each of these terms will be bounded later by our previous work. Now, for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{n_{S}}{n}\mathbb{E}|\widehat{V}_{S}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-V_{S}^{*,(1)}|\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{n})}\biggl|\frac{\widehat{r}_{S,(1)}}{\mathbb{E}{\{r_{S}(X)\}}}\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})^{2}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})^{2}\biggr|\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl|\frac{\widehat{r}_{S,(1)}}{r_{S}(X_{n})}\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})^{2}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})^{2}\}+\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{n})}\biggl\{\frac{\widehat{r}_{S,(1)}}{\mathbb{E}\{r_{S}(X)\}}-1\biggr\}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{n})}\bigl|\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})^{2}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})^{2}\bigr|\biggr]+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr]\mathbb{E}\biggl|\frac{\widehat{r}_{S,(1)}}{\mathbb{E}\{r_{S}(X)\}}-1\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{2|\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})|}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{n})}\bigl|\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr|+\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0pt+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr]|\mathcal{D}_{1}|^{-1/2}\mathrm{Var}^{1/2}\{\bar{r}_{S}(X)\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C}{c^{1/2}}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr]\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]+\frac{C^{1/2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1}|^{1/2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0pt+C\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr\}^{2}\bigr],

(66)

where, again, these terms are controlled by our earlier work. Indeed, combining (
65
) and (
66
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\biggl|\widehat{V}^{(1)}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\widehat{V}_{S}^{(1)}-V^{*,(1)}-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}V_{S}^{*,(1)}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq 2\biggl\{|\mathbb{S}|\mathcal{L}^{1/2}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})+\frac{C}{c^{1/2}}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{n}{n_{S}}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr]\biggr\}\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+|\mathbb{S}|\biggl(|\mathbb{S}|+\frac{Cn}{\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}}n_{S}}\biggr)\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]+2|\theta|\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\mathbb{E}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\}+\frac{C^{1/2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1}|^{1/2}}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{n}{n_{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathbb{S}|\biggl(1+\frac{C}{c^{1/2}}\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{n\lambda_{S}^{1/2}}{n_{S}}\biggr)\mathcal{L}^{1/2}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+|\mathbb{S}|\biggl(|\mathbb{S}|+\frac{Cn}{\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}}n_{S}}\biggr)\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}(X_{n})-\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{n})\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]+2|\theta|\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\mathbb{E}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\}+\frac{C^{1/2}}{|\mathcal{D}_{1}|^{1/2}}\biggl(\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\lambda_{S}n}{n_{S}}\biggr)\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3C|\mathbb{S}|\mathrm{Var}^{1/2}a(X)}{c^{1/2}\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}^{1/2}}\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\biggl\{\int(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\|a\|_{\infty}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\frac{4C|\mathbb{S}|^{2}}{\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}}\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\int(\widehat{\alpha}_{S,(1)}^{(M)}-\alpha_{S}^{(M)})^{2}f_{S}\biggr\}+\mathbb{E}\{(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)^{2}\}+\frac{4C^{1/2}\mathrm{Var}\,a(X)}{n^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq 8A^{M/2}D\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/2},

(67)

where the penultimate inequality follows from Proposition
12
and the final inequality follows from Proposition
14
and Theorem
15
.

We have now shown that our variance estimators can be approximated by their oracle counterparts, and the consistency of these estimators is thus established by showing that the oracle estimators
V^{*,(\ell)}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}V_{S}^{*,(\ell)}
are consistent. It is clear that they are unbiased estimators of
n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})
. Now using (
38
) and an analogous argument to (
44
) we have that

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{S}^{(M)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{M}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}:S_{m}=S}b_{M,\eta}(m)\|a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|a\|_{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{M}(2|\mathbb{S}|)^{m-1}b_{M,\eta}(m)\leq 2\|a\|_{\infty}3^{M}.

(68)

We therefore have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\biggl(V^{*,(1)}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}V_{S}^{*,(1)}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\biggl\{a(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)\biggr\}^{2}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{n}{n_{S}}\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|a\|_{\infty}^{4}}{|\mathcal{D}_{2}|}\biggl\{\bigl(1+2|\mathbb{S}|3^{M}\bigr)^{2}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{2n3^{M}}{cn_{S}}\biggr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1184\|a\|_{\infty}^{4}|\mathbb{S}|^{4}3^{4M}}{n\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}^{2}}\leq\frac{3^{4M}D^{2}}{n}.

(69)

Combining (
67
) and (
69
) with Markov’s inequality we thus see that we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl|\widehat{V}-n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl|\widehat{V}^{(1)}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\widehat{V}_{S}^{(1)}-n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq 8A^{M/2}D\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/2}+\frac{3^{2M}D}{n^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq 9A^{M/2}D\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/2}

(70)

so that our variance estimators are consistent.

With control over
\widehat{V}
we now proceed to prove the approximate normality of the standardised estimator
\widehat{\theta}
. To do this we will need to bound the variance of
\theta^{*,(M)}
below. Using the shorthand
v_{M}=n\mathrm{Var}(\theta^{*,(M)})
we have

\displaystyle v_{M}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\biggl(a(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)\biggr)+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{n}{n_{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{S}}\biggl(\frac{n\lambda_{S}}{n_{S}}-1\biggr)\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)^{2}}{\bar{r}_{S}(X)}\biggr\}\geq\biggl(1-\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl|\frac{n\lambda_{S}}{n_{S}}-1\biggr|\biggr)\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n-1}{n}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{(M)})\geq\frac{n-1}{n}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\geq\frac{n-1}{n}V_{0}.

(71)

For
\epsilon\in(0,1/2]
and
\tau>0
we have by elementary calculations that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\biggl\{\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)}{\widehat{V}^{1/2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\widehat{V}>0\}}\leq x\biggr)-\Phi(x)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\biggl\{\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)}{v_{M}^{1/2}}\leq x(1+\epsilon)\biggr)-\Phi(x)\biggr\}+\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\widehat{V}}{v_{M}}\geq(1+\epsilon)^{2}\biggr)+\mathbb{P}(\widehat{V}\leq 0)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\biggl\{\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}(\theta^{*,(M)}-\theta)}{v_{M}^{1/2}}\leq x(1+\epsilon)+\tau\biggr)-\Phi(x)\biggr\}+\mathbb{P}(n^{1/2}|\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)}|\geq\tau v_{M}^{1/2})

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\widehat{V}}{v_{M}}\geq(1+\epsilon)^{2}\biggr)+\mathbb{P}(\widehat{V}\leq 0)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\biggl\{\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}(\theta^{*,(M)}-\theta)}{v_{M}^{1/2}}\leq x\biggr)-\Phi(x)\biggr\}+\frac{\tau}{(2\pi)^{1/2}}+2\epsilon

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+\mathbb{P}(n^{1/2}|\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)}|\geq\tau v_{M}^{1/2})+\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\widehat{V}-v_{M}|\geq\epsilon v_{M}\bigr).

With similar control over the upper tail probabilities, writing
Z\sim N(0,1)
and taking
\tau=[\mathbb{E}\{n(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\}/v_{M}]^{1/3}
and
\epsilon=\min\{1/2,\mathbb{E}|\widehat{V}-v_{M}|/v_{M}\}
, we see by (
64
), (
70
) and (
71
) that

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)}{\widehat{V}^{1/2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\widehat{V}>0\}},

\displaystyle Z\biggr)-d_{\mathrm{K}}\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}(\theta^{*,(M)}-\theta)}{v_{M}^{1/2}},Z\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq 2(\tau+\epsilon)+\frac{\mathbb{E}\{n(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\}}{\tau^{2}v_{M}}+2\frac{\mathbb{E}|\widehat{V}-v_{M}|}{\epsilon v_{M}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\biggl(\frac{\mathbb{E}\{n(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\}}{v_{M}}\biggr)^{1/3}+2\biggl(\frac{\mathbb{E}|\widehat{V}-v_{M}|}{v_{M}}\biggr)+4\biggl(\frac{\mathbb{E}|\widehat{V}-v_{M}|}{v_{M}}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6A^{M/2}D}{V_{0}}\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/4}.

(72)

It now remains to prove that
\theta^{*,(M)}
is approximately normally distributed. Since this is the sum of independent random variables we can appeal to the Berry–Esseen theorem
(
Shevtsova, 2010
, e.g.)
, for which we bound the third absolute moments of the summands. For
i\in[n]
we have by (
68
) that

\displaystyle\beta_{i}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\frac{1}{(nv_{M})^{1/2}}\biggl\{a(X_{i})-\theta-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{i})\biggr\}\biggr|^{3}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(1+3^{M}|\mathbb{S}|)\|a\|_{\infty}}{(nv_{M})^{3/2}}\mathrm{Var}\biggl(a(X)-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X)\biggr).

Similarly, we have for
S\in\mathbb{S}
and
j\in[n_{S}]
that

\displaystyle\beta_{S,j}:=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl|\frac{n^{1/2}}{n_{S}v_{M}^{1/2}}\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{S,j})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}\biggr|^{3}\biggr]\leq\biggl(\frac{n}{n_{S}^{2}v_{M}}\biggr)^{3/2}\frac{2\|a\|_{\infty}3^{M}}{c}\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\frac{\alpha_{S}^{(M)}(X_{S,1})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}\biggr).

Thus, we have

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}(\theta^{*,(M)}-\theta)}{v_{M}^{1/2}},Z\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\beta_{S,j}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\|a\|_{\infty}}{(nv_{M})^{1/2}}\max\biggl\{1+3^{M}|\mathbb{S}|,\frac{3^{M}}{c}\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{n}{n_{S}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\|a\|_{\infty}}{(nv_{M})^{1/2}}3^{M}|\mathbb{S}|\biggl(1+\frac{1}{c\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}}\biggr).

It now follows from the above display and (
72
) that

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{K}}\biggl(\frac{n^{1/2}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta)}{\widehat{V}^{1/2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\widehat{V}>0\}},Z\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6A^{M/2}D}{V_{0}}\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/4}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+\frac{8\|a\|_{\infty}}{(nV_{0})^{1/2}}3^{M}|\mathbb{S}|\biggl(1+\frac{1}{c\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7A^{M/2}D}{V_{0}}\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/4},

as claimed.

∎

D.3
Proofs for Section
5

Proof of Theorem
17
.

The existence and uniqueness of
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
, the minimiser of
\mathcal{L}(\cdot,M_{\mathbb{S}})
, is established by using very similar bounds to those used in the proof of Proposition
6
. Given this minimiser, we may define

\alpha^{*}(x)=\frac{1}{M_{[d]}(x_{S_{0}})}\biggl\{a(x)-a_{S_{0}}(x_{S_{0}})-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{*}(x_{S})\biggr\}

and
h^{*}(x)=a_{S_{0}}(x_{S_{0}})-\theta+\alpha^{*}(x)
. Later we will use the fact that for
S\in\mathbb{S}
we have

\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{*}(X)|X_{S}\}=\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})}{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}

(73)

almost surely, which is a conditional version of our stationarity condition (
26
).

Next we establish the local asymptotic normality of the one-dimensional submodel
\{f_{t}:t\in\mathbb{R}\}
, where we use the shorthand
f_{t}
for
f_{th^{*}}
, and write
P_{N,t}
for the distribution of all of our data under
f_{N^{-1/2}t}
. Our inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{M_{\mathbb{S}}}
is defined by

\langle t_{1},t_{2}\rangle_{M_{\mathbb{S}}}=t_{1}t_{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\{a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-\theta\}^{2}+\frac{\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})^{2}}{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggr].

Almost exactly as in the proof of Theorem
8
, we have control over the behaviour of
f_{t}
for small values of
t
and, under
P_{N,0}
, we see that

\displaystyle\log\frac{dP_{N,t}}{dP_{N,0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega_{i}=S\}}\log\bigl(1+N^{-1/2}t(h^{*})_{S}(X_{i,S})\bigr)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=N^{-1/2}t\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega_{i}=S\}}(h^{*})_{S}(X_{i,S})-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega=S\}}(h^{*})_{S}(X_{S})^{2}\biggr\}+o_{p}(1).

Hence, we see that
\log\frac{dP_{N,t}}{dP_{N,0}}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\rightarrow}\sigma Z-(1/2)\sigma^{2}
where
Z\sim N(0,1)
and, using (
73
)

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=t^{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega=S\}}(h^{*})_{S}(X_{S})^{2}\biggr\}=t^{2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})\mathbb{E}\bigl\{(h^{*})_{S}(X_{S})^{2}|X_{S_{0}}\bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=t^{2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})\biggl[(h^{*})_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})^{2}+\bigl\{(h^{*})_{S}(X_{S})-(h^{*})_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})\bigr\}^{2}\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=t^{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl\{a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-\theta\bigr\}^{2}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})(\alpha^{*})_{S}(X_{S})^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=t^{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl\{a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-\theta\bigr\}h^{*}(X)+M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})\alpha^{*}(X)^{2}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})\alpha^{*}(X)\biggr]

\displaystyle=t^{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl\{a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-\theta\bigr\}h^{*}(X)+\alpha^{*}(X)\bigl\{a(X)-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})\bigr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=t^{2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl\{a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-\theta\bigr\}h^{*}(X)+h^{*}(X)\bigl\{a(X)-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})\bigr\}\biggr]=t^{2}\mathbb{E}\{a(X)h^{*}(X)\}.

(74)

The regularity of our sequence of parameters
\theta(f_{N^{-1/2}t})
follows from almost identical arguments to those used in the proof of Theorem
8
and, using the notation of
(
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, Chapter 3)
, we may write
\dot{\kappa}(t)=t\mathbb{E}\{a(X)h^{*}(X)\}
and
r_{N}=N^{1/2}
, where
\dot{\kappa}
is thought of as a map from
(\mathbb{R},\langle\cdot,\rangle_{M_{\mathbb{S}}})
to
\mathbb{R}
equipped with the usual Euclidean metric. The calculation (
74
) then shows that the adjoint
\dot{\kappa}^{*}
of
\dot{\kappa}
is simply the identity. The result now follows on applying
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996, Theorem 3.11.5)
and noting that

\displaystyle\|\dot{\kappa}^{*}

\displaystyle 1\|_{M_{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\{a(X)h^{*}(X)\}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\,a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggl\{a(X)-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})\biggr\}^{2}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})^{2}}{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggr]

which follows from the third line of (
74
).

∎

Proof of Proposition
18
.

Using the fact that
\mathbb{E}\{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})|X_{S_{0}}\}=0
almost surely, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigl|\mathbb{E}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}|\mathcal{D})\bigr|=\mathbb{E}\bigl|\mathbb{E}\bigl(\widehat{\theta}-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})\bigm|\mathcal{D}\bigr)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\widehat{a}_{S_{0}}(X)-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega_{i}=\mathbbm{1}_{[d]}\}}}{\widehat{M}_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggl\{a(X)-\widehat{a}_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 250.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega_{i}=\mathbbm{1}_{S}\}}}{\widehat{M}_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})\biggm|\mathcal{D}\biggr]\biggr|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{S_{0}}(X)-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})\bigr\}\biggl\{1-\frac{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}{\widehat{M}_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl\{\frac{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}{\widehat{M}_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}-\frac{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}{\widehat{M}_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggr\}\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})-\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})\bigr\}\biggm|\mathcal{D}\biggr]\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq(1+2|\mathbb{S}|)(a_{N}b_{N})^{1/2},

(75)

where the final inequality follows by the triangle and Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities.

Having bounded the conditional mean of
\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}
we now turn to its conditional absolute deviation. With the notation
\widehat{g}=a-\widehat{a}_{S_{0}}-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\widehat{\alpha}_{S}
and
g^{*}=a-a_{S_{0}}-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\alpha_{S}^{*}
, we start by defining

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl[\widehat{a}_{S_{0}}(X_{i})-a_{S_{0}}(X_{i})+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega_{i}=\mathbbm{1}_{[d]}\}}}{M_{[d]}(X_{i})}\biggl\{\widehat{g}(X_{i})-g^{*}(X_{i})+g^{*}(X_{i})\biggl(\frac{M_{[d]}(X_{i})}{\widehat{M}_{[d]}(X_{i})}-1\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 140.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega_{i}=\mathbbm{1}_{S}\}}}{M_{S}(X_{i})}\biggl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{i})-\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{i})+\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{i})\biggl(\frac{M_{S}(X_{i})}{\widehat{M}_{S}(X_{i})}-1\biggr)\biggr\}\biggr]

and

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\biggl[\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega_{i}=\mathbbm{1}_{[d]}\}}}{M_{[d]}(X_{i})}\bigl\{\widehat{g}(X_{i})-g^{*}(X_{i})\bigr\}\biggl\{\frac{M_{[d]}(X_{i})}{\widehat{M}_{[d]}(X_{i})}-1\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega_{i}=\mathbbm{1}_{S}\}}}{M_{S}(X_{i})}\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{i})-\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{i})\bigr\}\biggl\{\frac{M_{S}(X_{i})}{\widehat{M}_{S}(X_{i})}-1\biggr\}\biggr]

so that we have
\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}=L+Q
. Similarly to in our bound on the conditional mean above, we have

\mathbb{E}\bigl|Q-\mathbb{E}(Q|\mathcal{D})\bigr|\leq 2\mathbb{E}|Q|\leq(1+2|\mathbb{S}|)(a_{N}b_{N})^{1/2}.

(76)

It remains to control

\displaystyle N

\displaystyle\bigl\{\mathbb{E}\bigl|L-\mathbb{E}(L|\mathcal{D})\bigr|\bigr\}^{2}\leq N\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\mathrm{Var}(L|\mathcal{D})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\widehat{a}_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega=\mathbbm{1}_{[d]}\}}}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggl\{\widehat{g}(X)-g^{*}(X)+g^{*}(X)\biggl(\frac{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}{\widehat{M}_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}-1\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\Omega=\mathbbm{1}_{S}\}}}{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})-\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})+\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})\biggl(\frac{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}{\widehat{M}_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}-1\biggr)\biggr\}\biggm|\mathcal{D},\mathbf{X}_{S_{0}}\biggr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq(2+5|\mathbb{S}|)\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{1+\frac{1}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggr\}\bigl\{\widehat{a}_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})\bigr\}^{2}+\frac{g^{*}(X)^{2}}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggl\{\frac{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}{\widehat{M}_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}-1\biggr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl\{\frac{1}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}+\frac{1}{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggr\}\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})-\alpha_{S}^{*}(X_{S})\bigr\}^{2}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X)^{2}}{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggl\{\frac{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}{\widehat{M}_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}-1\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(2+5|\mathbb{S}|)(1+|\mathbb{S}|)a_{N}}{\inf_{x_{S_{0}}}\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}M_{S}(x_{S_{0}})}+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{g^{*}(X)^{2}}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X)^{2}}{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggr\}\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}\biggl\{\frac{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}{\widehat{M}_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}-1\biggr\}^{2}\biggr].

(77)

Now, since
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}
is the minimiser of
\mathcal{L}(\cdot,M_{\mathbb{S}})
, and since it is minimised for each value of
X_{S_{0}}
separately, we have that

\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{g^{*}(X)^{2}}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{\alpha_{S}^{*}(X)^{2}}{M_{S}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggm|X_{S_{0}}\biggr\}\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\{a(X)-a_{S_{0}}(X_{S_{0}})\}^{2}}{M_{[d]}(X_{S_{0}})}\biggm|X_{S_{0}}\biggr],

which is the value that we would get by choosing
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}(\cdot,X_{S_{0}})=0
. It therefore follows from (
75
), (
76
) and (
77
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigl|\mathbb{E}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}|\mathcal{D},\mathbf{X}_{S_{0}})\bigr|+\mathbb{E}\bigl|Q-\mathbb{E}(Q|\mathcal{D},\mathbf{X}_{S_{0}})\bigr|+\mathbb{E}\bigl|L-\mathbb{E}(L|\mathcal{D},\mathbf{X}_{S_{0}})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2(1+2|\mathbb{S}|)(a_{N}b_{N})^{1/2}+\frac{1}{\inf_{x_{S_{0}}}\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}_{+}}M_{S}(x_{S_{0}})^{1/2}}\biggl\{\frac{(2+5|\mathbb{S}|)a_{N}^{1/2}}{N^{1/2}}+\frac{\|a\|_{\infty}b_{N}^{1/2}}{N^{1/2}}\biggr\},

as required.

∎

Proof of Theorem
19
.

Since
H_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}
is a closed subspace of
H_{\mathbb{S}}
, the existence and uniqueness of the minimiser
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}
over
H_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}
follows from almost identical arguments to those given in the proof of Proposition
6
for minimisation over
H_{\mathbb{S}}
. We begin by establishing an analogue of our stationarity condition (
26
) that will be useful later in the proof. For any
S\in\mathbb{S},\epsilon_{S}\in H_{S}^{\perp}
and
\eta\in\mathbb{R}
, we may consider perturbing
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}
by replacing
\alpha_{S}^{\perp}
by
\alpha_{S}^{\perp}+\eta\epsilon_{S}
. By the minimality of
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}
we must have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\alpha^{\perp}(X)-\eta\epsilon_{S}(X_{S})\bigr)-\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\alpha^{\perp}(X)\bigr)+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{\{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})+\eta\epsilon_{S}(X_{S})\}^{2}-\{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})\}^{2}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggr]

\displaystyle=-2\eta\mathbb{E}\biggl[\epsilon_{S}(X_{S})\biggl\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)-\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggr\}\biggr]+O(\eta^{2})

\displaystyle=-2\eta\mathbb{E}\biggl[\epsilon_{S}(X_{S})\biggl\{(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S})-\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggr\}\biggr]+O(\eta^{2}),

as
\eta\rightarrow 0
, where we recall our notational convention that
(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S})=\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)|X_{S}\}
. As
\epsilon_{S}\in H_{S}^{\perp}
was arbitrary, using the fact that the orthogonal complement of
H_{S}^{\perp}
within
H_{S}
is given by the span of
\Psi_{S}
, we see that there exist
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mu_{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{k_{S}}
such that

(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S})-\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}=\mu+\mu_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S})

(78)

almost surely.

By almost identical arguments to those applied in the proof of Theorem
8
, we have control over the behaviour of
f_{t\alpha^{\perp}}
for small values of
t
. We therefore see that, under
P_{n,0,0}
, we have

\displaystyle\log

\displaystyle\frac{dP_{n,t,v_{\mathbb{S}}}}{dP_{n,0,0}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\bigl(1+n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{\perp}(X_{i})\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\log\frac{\exp\bigl(n^{-1/2}v_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,j})\bigr)\bigl\{1+n^{-1/2}t(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,j})\bigr\}\int e^{w_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}}f}{\int e^{(w_{S}+n^{-1/2}v_{S})^{T}\Psi_{S}}f(1+tn^{-1/2}\alpha^{\perp})}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}t\alpha^{\perp}(X_{i})-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathrm{Var}\bigl(v_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1})+t(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.0pt+n^{-1/2}\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\biggl[v_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,j})+t(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,j})-\mathbb{E}\bigl\{v_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,j})+t(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,j})\bigr\}\biggr]+o_{p}(1).

We therefore see that
\log\frac{dP_{n,t,v_{\mathbb{S}}}}{dP_{n,0,0}}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{\rightarrow}\|(t,v_{\mathbb{S}})\|_{\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}}Z-(1/2)\|(t,v_{\mathbb{S}})\|_{\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}
, where
Z\sim N(0,1)
and where
\|\cdot\|_{\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}}
is the norm associated to the inner product

\displaystyle\langle(t,v_{\mathbb{S}}),(s,u_{\mathbb{S}})\rangle_{\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}}

\displaystyle=st\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(v_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1})+t(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,1}),u_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1})+s(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr),

which establishes the local asymptotic normality of this sequence of experiments.

The regularity of our sequence of parameters
\theta(f_{n^{-1/2}t\alpha^{\perp}})
follows from almost identical arguments to those used in the proof of Theorem
8
and, using the notation of
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996, Chapter 3)
, we may write
\dot{\kappa}(t,v_{\mathbb{S}})=t\mathbb{E}\{a(X)\alpha^{\perp}(X)\}
and
r_{N}=n^{1/2}
, where
\dot{\kappa}
is thought of as a map from
(\mathbb{R}^{1+k_{\mathbb{S}}},\langle\cdot,\rangle_{\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}})
to
\mathbb{R}
equipped with the usual Euclidean metric. To find the adjoint
\dot{\kappa}^{*}
of
\dot{\kappa}
we aim to find
(t^{*},v_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{1+k_{\mathbb{S}}}
such that
\langle(t^{*},v_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}),(t,v_{\mathbb{S}})\rangle_{\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}}=\dot{\kappa}(t,v_{\mathbb{S}})
for all
(t,v_{\mathbb{S}})\in\mathbb{R}^{1+k_{\mathbb{S}}}
. Since we assume that the functions
\Psi_{S}
are linearly independent, for each
S\in\mathbb{S}
we may define

v_{S}^{*}=-\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)^{-1}\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1}),(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)

so that
\mathrm{Cov}((\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,1})+(v_{S}^{*})^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1}),\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1}))=0
. Using our stationarity condition (
78
) and the fact that
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}
, we have for almost all
x_{S}
that

\displaystyle(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(x_{S})+(v_{S}^{*})^{T}\Psi_{S}(x_{S})=\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(x_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}+\mu+\mu_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(x_{S})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)^{-1}\mathrm{Cov}\biggl(\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1}),\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S,1})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}+\mu+\mu_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1})\biggr)\Psi_{S}(x_{S})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(x_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}+\mu+\mu_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(x_{S})-\frac{1}{\lambda_{S}}\mathrm{Cov}\bigl(\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\Psi_{S}(X_{S})\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})\bigr\}-\mu_{S}^{T}\Psi_{S}(x_{S})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(x_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(x_{S})}+\mu

Thus, for any
(t,v_{\mathbb{S}})\in\mathbb{R}^{1+k_{\mathbb{S}}}
we may write that

\displaystyle\langle(1,v_{\mathbb{S}}^{*}),(t,v_{\mathbb{S}})\rangle_{\Psi_{\mathbb{S}}}

\displaystyle=t\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathrm{Cov}\bigl((\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,1})+(v_{S}^{*})^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S,1}),t(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,1})\bigr)

\displaystyle=t\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}+t\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\lambda_{S}\mathrm{Cov}\biggl(\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S,1})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})},(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S,1})\biggr)

\displaystyle=t\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}+t\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=t\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}+t\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})\alpha^{\perp}(X)\bigr\}=t\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)a(X)\}=\dot{\kappa}(t,v_{\mathbb{S}}),

as required. This shows that the adjoint is given by
\dot{\kappa}^{*}:t\mapsto(t,tv_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})
. The result now follows on using the above calculation and (
78
) to see that

\displaystyle\|(1,v_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\|_{\Psi_{S}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\dot{\kappa}(1,v_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})=\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})(\alpha^{\perp})_{S}(X_{S})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})\biggl\{\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}+\mu-(v_{S}^{*})^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S})\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\alpha^{\perp}(X)^{2}\}+\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})^{2}}{\lambda_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggr\}

and applying
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996, Theorem 3.11.5)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
20
.

Since
\theta^{*}
is unbiased and independent of
\mathcal{D}
, and since
\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}\in H_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}
, we may control the first moment by writing

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl|\mathbb{E}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}|\mathcal{D})\bigr|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl|\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{i})+\frac{1}{n_{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\frac{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S,j})}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}\biggm|\mathcal{D}\biggr]\biggr|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl|\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S})}-1\biggr\}\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})\biggr]\biggr|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl|\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S})}-1\biggr\}\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})-\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 75.0pt+\biggl\{\frac{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S})}-1-w_{S}^{0}+\widehat{w}_{S}^{0}-(w_{S}-\widehat{w}_{S})^{T}\Psi_{S}(X_{S})\biggr\}\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})\biggr]\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{S}|\{(a_{n}b_{n})^{1/2}+b_{n}\},

which is negligible under our hypotheses. It now remains to control the fluctuations of
\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}
about its conditional mean. To this end, we define

\displaystyle L=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\biggl\{\frac{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S,j})-\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S,j})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}+\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S,j})\biggl(\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{i})-\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{i})\bigr\}\biggr]

and

Q=\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\frac{1}{n_{S}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S,j})-\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S,j})\bigr\}\biggl(\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}-\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,j})}\biggr)

so that
\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*}=L+Q
. With similar arguments to those employed for the conditional mean, we see that

\mathbb{E}|Q-\mathbb{E}(Q|\mathcal{D})|\leq 2\mathbb{E}|Q|\leq 2\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl|\bigl\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})-\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})\bigr\}\biggl(\frac{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S})}-1\biggr)\biggr|\leq 2|\mathbb{S}|(a_{n}b_{n})^{1/2}

is negligible. On the other hand, we have that

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathbb{E}|

\displaystyle L-\mathbb{E}(L|\mathcal{D})|\bigr)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\{\mathrm{Var}(L|\mathcal{D})\}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{S}|\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{n_{S}}\mathrm{Var}\biggl\{\frac{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S,1})-\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S,1})}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}+\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S,1})\biggl(\frac{1}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}-\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S,1})}\biggr)\biggm|\mathcal{D}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X)-\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X)\bigm|\mathcal{D}\bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{S}|\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{2}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}+\frac{1}{n}\biggr\}\{\widehat{\alpha}_{S}(X_{S})-\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})\}^{2}+\frac{\alpha_{S}^{\perp}(X_{S})^{2}}{n_{S}\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}\biggl\{\frac{\bar{r}_{S}(X_{S})}{\widehat{r}_{S}(X_{S})}-1\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{S}|^{2}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{2}{n_{S}}+\frac{1}{n}\biggr)a_{n}+\frac{b_{n}}{n_{S}}\biggr\},

as required. This completes the proof.
∎

D.4
Proofs for Section
A

Proof of Proposition
22
.

Write

\displaystyle\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}\widehat{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{i_{1}},\ldots,X_{i_{m+1}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}a(X_{i_{1}})\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S_{1}}(X_{i_{2}}-X_{i_{1}})}{\widehat{f}_{S_{1},h}(X_{i_{1}})}-1\biggr\}\ldots\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S_{m}}(X_{i_{m+1}}-X_{i_{m}})}{\widehat{f}_{S_{m},h}(X_{i_{m}})}-1\biggr\}

so that
\check{\theta}_{h}^{M}=\widehat{\theta}^{\mathrm{CC}}+\sum_{m=1}^{M}(-1)^{m}\sum_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}}v_{(S_{1},\ldots,S_{m-1})}b_{M,\eta}(m)\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}
. In this proof we will first consider
\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}
for each
m\in[M]
and
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
separately, providing progressively simpler approximations, before combining these approximations to prove the result.

The main technical difficulty is in accounting for the random fluctuations of the density estimators
\widehat{f}_{S,h}
about their means. To ease notation we will write
K_{i,j}^{S}=\frac{K_{h}^{S}(X_{i}-X_{j})}{f_{S,h}(X_{i})}-1
for any
i,j\in[n]
and
S\in\mathbb{S}
and
L_{i,j}^{S}=\frac{K_{h}^{S}(X_{i}-X_{S,j})}{f_{S,h}(X_{i})}-1
for any
i\in[n]
,
S\in\mathbb{S}
and
j\in[n_{S}]
. For
i\in[n]
and
S\in\mathbb{S}
, we now write

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i}^{S}=\frac{\widehat{f}_{S,h}(X_{i})}{f_{S,h}(X_{i})}-1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n+n_{S}}\biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{n}\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(X_{i}-X_{j})}{f_{S,h}(X_{i})}-1\biggr\}+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(X_{i}-X_{S,j})}{f_{S,h}(X_{i})}-1\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n+n_{S}}\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{n}K_{i,j}^{S}+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{S}}L_{i,j}^{S}\biggr).

Our first step in the direction of giving simple approximations to
\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}
is to argue that the denominators
\widehat{f}_{S_{j}}(X_{i_{j}})=(1+\epsilon_{i_{j}}^{S_{j}})f_{S_{j},h}(X_{i_{j}})
in
\widehat{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}
can be replaced by
(1-\epsilon_{i_{j}}^{S_{j}})^{-1}f_{S_{j},h}(X_{i_{j}})
up to a negligible error. To this end, for
\ell\in[m+1]
, write

\displaystyle\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}a(X_{i_{1}})\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S_{1}}(X_{i_{2}}-X_{i_{1}})}{(1-\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{S_{1}})^{-1}f_{h}^{S_{1}}(X_{i_{1}})}-1\biggr\}\ldots\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S_{\ell-1}}(X_{i_{\ell}}-X_{i_{\ell-1}})}{(1-\epsilon_{i_{\ell-1}}^{S_{\ell-1}})^{-1}f_{h}^{S_{\ell-1}}(X_{i_{\ell-1}})}-1\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 140.0pt\times\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S_{\ell}}(X_{i_{\ell+1}}-X_{i_{\ell}})}{(1+\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell}})f_{h}^{S_{\ell}}(X_{i_{\ell}})}-1\biggr\}\ldots\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S_{m}}(X_{i_{m+1}}-X_{i_{m}})}{(1+\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{S_{m}})f_{h}^{S_{m}}(X_{i_{m}})}-1\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}a(X_{i_{1}})\bigl\{K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}}-(1+K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}})\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{S_{1}}\bigr\}\ldots\bigl\{K_{i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell-1}}-(1+K_{i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell-1}})\epsilon_{i_{\ell-1}}^{S_{\ell-1}}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 140.0pt\times\biggl(\frac{K_{i_{\ell},i_{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}}-\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell}}}{1+\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell}}}\biggr)\ldots\biggl(\frac{K_{i_{m},i_{m+1}}^{S_{m}}-\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{S_{m}}}{1+\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{S_{m}}}\biggr)

so that
\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(1)}=\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}
and each successive
\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell+1)}
is the result of starting with
\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)}
and making the replacement described above in the next factor in the summands. Now for
\ell\in[m]
we use the fact that, since
K
is supported on
[-1/2,1/2]^{d}
and symmetric, we have
f_{S,h}(x)\geq c_{0}/2^{d}
to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{(\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell+1)}-\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)})^{2}\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\biggl[\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}a(X_{i_{1}})\bigl\{K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}}-(1+K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}})\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{S_{1}}\bigr\}\ldots\bigl\{K_{i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell-1}}-(1+K_{i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell-1}})\epsilon_{i_{\ell-1}}^{S_{\ell-1}}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt\times\frac{(\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell}})^{2}}{1+\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell}}}\bigl(K_{i_{\ell},i_{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}}+1\bigr)\biggl(\frac{K_{i_{\ell+1},i_{\ell+2}}^{S_{\ell+1}}-\epsilon_{i_{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell+1}}}{1+\epsilon_{i_{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell+1}}}\biggr)\ldots\biggl(\frac{K_{i_{m},i_{m+1}}^{S_{m}}-\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{S_{m}}}{1+\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{S_{m}}}\biggr)\biggr]^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[a(X_{1})^{2}\bigl\{K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}}-(1+K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}})\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{S_{1}}\bigr\}^{2}\ldots\bigl\{K_{i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell-1}}-(1+K_{i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell-1}})\epsilon_{i_{\ell-1}}^{S_{\ell-1}}\bigr\}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt\times\frac{(\epsilon_{\ell}^{S_{\ell}})^{4}}{(1+\epsilon_{\ell}^{S_{\ell}})^{2}}\bigl(K_{\ell,{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}}+1\bigr)^{2}\biggl(\frac{K_{{\ell+1},{\ell+2}}^{S_{\ell+1}}-\epsilon_{{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell+1}}}{1+\epsilon_{{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell+1}}}\biggr)^{2}\ldots\biggl(\frac{K_{m,{m+1}}^{S_{m}}-\epsilon_{m}^{S_{m}}}{1+\epsilon_{m}^{S_{m}}}\biggr)^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2(\ell-1)}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\prod_{j=1}^{\ell-1}(1+|\epsilon_{j}^{S_{j}}|)^{2}\biggr\}(\epsilon_{\ell}^{S_{\ell}})^{4}\prod_{j=\ell}^{m}\max\biggl\{1,\frac{K_{h}^{S_{j}}(X_{j+1}-X_{j})^{2}}{\widehat{f}_{S_{j}}(X_{j})^{2}}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2(\ell-1)}\max_{j\in[\ell-1]}\mathbb{E}^{\frac{\ell-1}{m+1}}\bigl\{(1+|\epsilon_{j}^{S_{j}}|)^{2(m+1)}\bigr\}\times\mathbb{E}^{1/(m+1)}\bigl\{(\epsilon_{\ell}^{S_{\ell}})^{4(m+1)}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.0pt\times\max_{j=\ell,\ldots,m}\mathbb{E}^{(m-\ell+1)/(m+1)}\biggl[\max\biggl\{1,\frac{K_{h}^{S_{j}}(X_{j+1}-X_{j})^{2(m+1)}}{\widehat{f}_{S_{j}}(X_{j})^{2(m+1)}}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2(\ell-1)}\biggl[1+\max_{j\in[\ell-1]}\mathbb{E}^{\frac{\ell-1}{m+1}}\bigl\{|\epsilon_{j}^{S_{j}}|^{2(m+1)}\bigr\}\biggr]\times\mathbb{E}^{1/(m+1)}\bigl\{(\epsilon_{\ell}^{S_{\ell}})^{4(m+1)}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 130.0pt\times\max_{j=\ell,\ldots,m}\mathbb{E}^{(m-\ell+1)/(m+1)}\biggl[\max\biggl\{1,\frac{K_{h}^{S_{j}}(X_{j+1}-X_{j})^{2(m+1)}}{\widehat{f}_{S_{j}}(X_{j})^{2(m+1)}}\biggr\}\biggr]

(79)

We now bound each expectation appearing in the final line separately. By Rosenthal’s inequality
(e.g.
Boucheron, Lugosi and Massart, 2013
, Theorem 15.11)
, if we write
\kappa=e^{1/2}/(2(e^{1/2}-1))
, we have for any integer
q\geq 2
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1/q}(|\epsilon_{j}^{S_{j}}|^{q})

\displaystyle\leq 2^{1+1/q}\biggl\{(6\kappa q)^{1/2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}(n+n_{S_{j}})h^{|S_{j}|}}\biggr)^{1/2}+2q\kappa\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}(n+n_{S_{j}})h^{|S_{j}|}}\biggr)^{1-1/q}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 250.0pt+\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}(n+n_{S_{j}})h^{|S_{j}|}}

\displaystyle\leq 24q\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}(n+n_{S_{j}})h^{|S_{j}|}}\biggr)^{1/2}.

(80)

Now since
(n+n_{S_{j}})\widehat{f}_{S_{j}}(X_{j})\geq K_{h}^{S_{j}}(X_{j+1}-X_{j})
we have by Hoeffding’s inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\biggl[1\vee\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S_{j}}(X_{j+1}-X_{j})}{\widehat{f}_{S_{j}}(X_{j})}\biggr\}^{2(m+1)}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq(n+n_{S_{j}})^{2(m+1)}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{f}_{S_{j}}(X_{j})\leq c_{0}/2^{d+1})+\Bigl(\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{|S_{j}|}}\Bigr)^{2(m+1)}

\displaystyle\leq(n+n_{S_{j}})^{2(m+1)}\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\widehat{f}_{S_{j}}(X_{j})}{f_{S_{j},h}(X_{j})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\biggr)+\Bigl(\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{|S_{j}|}}\Bigr)^{2(m+1)}

\displaystyle\leq(n+n_{S_{j}})^{2(m+1)}\mathbb{P}\biggl(\sum_{i\neq j}K_{j,i}^{S_{j}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S_{j}}}L_{j,i}^{S_{j}}\leq-\frac{n+n_{S_{j}}}{2}(1-2^{-5})\biggr)+\Bigl(\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{|S_{j}|}}\Bigr)^{2(m+1)}

\displaystyle\leq(n+n_{S_{j}})^{2(m+1)}\exp\biggl(-\frac{c_{0}^{2}h^{2|S_{j}|}(n+n_{S_{j}})}{2^{2d+2}\|K\|_{\infty}^{2}}\biggr)+\Bigl(\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{|S_{j}|}}\Bigr)^{2(m+1)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Bigl(\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{|S_{j}|}}\Bigr)^{2(m+1)},

(81)

where the third and final inequalities follow from our assumed lower bound (
15
) on
n
.
It now follows from (
79
), (
80
) and (
81
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{(\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell+1)}

\displaystyle-\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)})^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\{96(m+1)\}^{4}}{n^{2}}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2m+2}\biggl[1+\biggl\{48(m+1)\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}nh^{d}}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggr\}^{2(\ell-1)}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\{96(m+1)\}^{4}}{n^{2}}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d+1}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2m+2}

(82)

where the final line again follows from (
15
). By writing
\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m+1)}-\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}(\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell+1)}-\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)})
and using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality we can use (
82
) to show that we can approximate
\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}
by
\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m+1)}
with negligible mean-squared error.

We may write

\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m+1)}=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}a(X_{i_{1}})\bigl\{K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}}-(1+K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}})\epsilon_{i_{1}}^{S_{1}}\bigr\}\ldots\bigl\{K_{i_{m},i_{m+1}}^{S_{m}}-(1+K_{i_{m},i_{m+1}}^{S_{m}})\epsilon_{i_{m}}^{S_{m}}\bigr\}.

We now expand these brackets and argue that any term containing two or more
\epsilon_{i_{j}}^{S_{j}}
factors can be neglected. To proceed, we define

\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}k_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{i_{1}},\ldots,X_{i_{m+1}})=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}a(X_{i_{1}})K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}}\ldots K_{i_{m},i_{m+1}}^{S_{m}}

and for
\ell\in[m]
we define

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}a(X_{i_{1}})K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}}\ldots K_{i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell-1}}(1+K_{i_{\ell},i_{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}})\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell}}K_{i_{\ell+1},i_{\ell+2}}^{S_{\ell+1}}\ldots K_{i_{m},i_{m+1}}^{S_{m}}.

Our aim now is to show that
\widehat{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m+1)}
can be approximated by
\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)}
, where there is at most one
\epsilon_{i_{j}}^{S_{j}}
factor per term. Indeed, using (
80
) we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\biggl(\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m+1)}-\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\max_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}:\|\alpha\|_{1}\geq 2}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}(K_{\ell,{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}})^{2(1-\alpha_{\ell})}\{(1+K_{\ell,{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}})\epsilon_{\ell}^{S_{\ell}}\}^{2\alpha_{\ell}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\max_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}:\|\alpha\|_{1}\geq 2}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\biggl\{\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}(K_{\ell,{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}})^{4(1-\alpha_{\ell})}(1+K_{\ell,{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}})^{4\alpha_{\ell}}\biggr\}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\biggl\{\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}(\epsilon_{\ell}^{S_{\ell}})^{4\alpha_{\ell}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\biggl[\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}\biggl\{1\vee\frac{K_{h}^{S_{\ell}}(X_{{\ell+1}}-X_{\ell})}{f_{h}^{S_{\ell}}(X_{{\ell}})}\biggr\}^{4}\biggr]\max_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}:\|\alpha\|_{1}\geq 2}\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}^{1/(2\|\alpha\|_{1})}\biggl[(\epsilon_{\ell}^{S_{\ell}})^{4\|\alpha\|_{1}\alpha_{\ell}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2m}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2m}\max_{A=2,\ldots,m}\biggl\{96A\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{nc_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggr\}^{2A}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2m+20}3^{4}m^{4}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{n^{2}}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2m+2},

(83)

where we use (
15
) for the final inequality.

We now consider the different parts of
\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell}}
separately in order to approximate each
\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)}
by one- and two-sample
U
-statistics that can be more easily understood. For a given
\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}
we write

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n+n_{S_{\ell}}}\biggl[\sum_{i\in[n]\setminus\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m+1}\}}K_{i_{\ell},i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S_{\ell}}}L_{i_{\ell},i}+\sum_{j=1}^{m+1}K_{i_{\ell},i_{j}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell},(1)}+\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell},(2)}+\frac{1}{n+n_{S_{\ell}}}\sum_{j=1}^{m+1}K_{i_{\ell},i_{j}}.

Using the above definitions we also write

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(j)}=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}a(X_{i_{1}})K_{i_{1},i_{2}}^{S_{1}}\ldots K_{i_{\ell-1},i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell-1}}(1+K_{i_{\ell},i_{\ell+1}}^{S_{\ell}})\epsilon_{i_{\ell}}^{S_{\ell},(j)}K_{i_{\ell+1},i_{\ell+2}}^{S_{\ell+1}}\ldots K_{i_{m},i_{m+1}}^{S_{m}}

for
j=1,2
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{(\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell)}-\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(1)}-\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(2)})^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(m+1)\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{(n+n_{S_{\ell}})^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m+1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[(K_{1,2}^{S_{1}})^{2}\ldots(K_{\ell-1,\ell}^{S_{\ell-1}})^{2}(1+K_{\ell,\ell+1}^{S_{\ell}})^{2}(K_{\ell+1,\ell+2}^{S_{\ell+1}})^{2}\ldots(K_{m,m+1}^{S_{m}})^{2}(K_{\ell,j}^{S_{\ell}})^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{(n+n_{S_{\ell}})^{2}}\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}^{\frac{m}{m+1}}\{(K_{1,2}^{S})^{2(m+1)}\}\times\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}^{\frac{1}{m+1}}\{(1+K_{1,2}^{S})^{2(m+1)}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{(n+n_{S_{\ell}})^{2}}\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}[\{1\vee(1+(K_{1,2}^{S})\}^{2(m+1)}]\leq\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{n^{2}}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2m+2}.

(84)

Now for
\ell\in[m-1]
we notice that
\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(1)}
is a degenerate
U
-statistic. Thus, letting
H\sim\mathrm{Hypergeometric}(n,m+2,m+2)
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{(\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(1)})^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(H\geq 2)\mathbb{E}\bigl[a(X_{1})^{2}(K_{1,2}^{S_{1}})^{2}\ldots(K_{\ell-1,\ell}^{S_{\ell-1}})^{2}(1+K_{\ell,\ell+1}^{S_{\ell}})^{2}(K_{\ell+1,\ell+2}^{S_{\ell+1}})^{2}\ldots(K_{m,m+1}^{S_{m}})^{2}(K_{\ell,m+2}^{S_{\ell}})^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(H\geq 2)\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{m+1}=\mathbb{P}(H(H-1)\geq 2)\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{m+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\{H(H-1)\}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{m+1}=\frac{(m+2)^{2}(m+1)^{2}}{2n(n-1)}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{m+1}.

(85)

On the other hand, when
\ell=m
, we see that

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}-\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m),(1)}=\frac{1}{(n)_{m+2}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+2}}k_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{i_{1}},\ldots,X_{i_{m+1}})\biggl\{1-\frac{n-m-1}{n+n_{S_{m}}}(1+K_{i_{m},i_{m+2}}^{S_{m}})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{S_{m}}+m+1}{n+n_{S_{m}}}\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}k_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{i_{1}},\ldots,X_{i_{m+1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-\frac{n-m-1}{n+n_{S_{m}}}\frac{1}{(n)_{m+2}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+2}}k_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{i_{1}},\ldots,X_{i_{m+1}})K_{i_{m},i_{m+2}}^{S_{m}},

where the second term in the above line is a degenerate
U
-statistic. Replacing the first term by it linearisation and arguing as in (
85
) we therefore have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}-\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m),(1)}-\frac{n_{S_{m}}+m+1}{n+n_{S_{m}}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{i})\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m^{2}(m+1)^{2}}{2n(n-1)}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{m}+\frac{(m+1)^{2}(m+2)^{2}}{2n(n-1)}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{m+1}.

(86)

We have now seen that
\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(1)}
may be approximated by an average of independent and identically distributed random variables. We turn our attention to the
\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(2)}
terms. These are now two-sample
U
-statistics rather than standard
U
-statistics, but similar arguments to those above still apply. For
\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m),(2)}
we can use Lemma A(iii) of
Serfling (1980)
to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(m),(2)}-\frac{1}{n+n_{S_{m}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S_{m}}}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{S_{m},i})\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{S_{m}}}{(n+n_{S_{m}})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\frac{1}{(n)_{m+1}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{m+1}}k_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{i_{1}},\ldots,X_{i_{m}},X_{S_{m},1})(1+K_{i_{m},i_{m+1}}^{S_{m}})-\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{S_{m},1})\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m+1}{(n+n_{S_{m}})n}\mathbb{E}\biggl[k_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m},X_{S_{m},1})^{2}(1+K_{m,{m+1}}^{S_{m}})^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m+1}{(n+n_{S_{m}})n}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{m+1}.

(87)

On the other hand, when
\ell\in[m-1]
we can see that
\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(2)}
is degenerate, so that

\mathbb{E}\{(\tilde{\theta}^{\mathbf{S},(\ell),(2)})^{2}\}\leq\frac{m+1}{(n+n_{S_{\ell}})n}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{m+1}.

(88)

From (
82
), (
83
), (
84
), (
85
), (
86
), (
87
) and (
88
) we have now established that there exists a universal constant
C_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\check{\theta}^{\mathbf{S}}-\frac{n_{S_{m}}+m+1}{n+n_{S_{m}}}\frac{1}{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{i})+\frac{n_{S_{m}}}{n+n_{S_{m}}}\frac{1}{n_{S_{m}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{S_{m}}}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X_{S_{m},i})\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\min(n,\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}}n_{S})^{2}}\biggl(\frac{C_{1}2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2m+2}.

It follows from (
44
) that there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\{(\check{\theta}_{h}^{M}-\theta_{h}^{*,M})^{2}\}\leq\frac{\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\min(n,\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}}n_{S})^{2}}\biggl(\frac{C2^{d}\|K\|_{\infty}}{c_{0}h^{d}}\biggr)^{2M+2},

as required.
∎

Proof of Proposition
23
.

We first deal with the minor technical point of approximating
n_{S}/n
by
\lambda_{S}
for
S\in\mathbb{S}
. For
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
write
w_{\mathbf{S}}=\prod_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}/(1+\lambda_{j})
. It is straightforward to see that

|w_{\mathbf{S}}-v_{\mathbf{S}}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{|n\lambda_{S_{j}}-n_{S_{j}}|}{(1+\lambda_{S_{j}})(n+n_{S_{j}})}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n_{S_{j}}}{(1+\lambda_{S_{j}})(n+n_{S_{j}})}\leq\frac{m}{n}.

Using the fact that both
\theta_{h}^{*,M}
and
\theta^{*,M}
are averages of independent and identically distributed random variables and using (
44
) we can see therefore that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{(\theta_{h}^{*,M}-\theta^{*,(M)})^{2}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{M+2}}{\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}^{+}}n_{S}}\max_{m\in[M]}\max_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\{v_{\mathbf{S}}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{M+2}}{\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}^{+}}n_{S}}\max_{m\in[M]}\max_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}}\Bigl\{\mathbb{E}\bigl[\{w_{\mathbf{S}}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\}^{2}\bigr]+(v_{\mathbf{S}}-w_{\mathbf{S}})^{2}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{M+2}}{\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}^{+}}n_{S}}\max_{m\in[M]}\max_{\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}}\Bigl\{\mathbb{E}\bigl[\{w_{\mathbf{S}}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)-\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\}^{2}\bigr]+\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}\Bigr\},

(89)

and we turn our attention to the error in using
w_{\mathbf{S}}\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(x)
to approximate
\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x)
. We will write
a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}=w_{\mathbf{S}}^{-1}\bar{a}_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}
so that we have

a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x_{S})=\mathbb{E}\{a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)|X_{S}=x_{S}\}.

First, we will control the smoothness of the functions
a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}
. Using the fact that
\|a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|a\|_{\infty}
, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
,
S\in\mathbb{S}\setminus\{S_{m}\}
and
x,x^{\prime}\in[0,1]^{d}
we have

\displaystyle|a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x)-a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m)}(x^{\prime})|=\bigl|\mathbb{E}\bigl\{a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)|X_{S}=x_{S}\bigr\}-\mathbb{E}\bigl\{a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X^{\prime})|X_{S}^{\prime}=x_{S}^{\prime}\bigr\}\bigr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\int\biggl[\{a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S},x_{S^{c}})-a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S}^{\prime},x_{S^{c}})\}\frac{f(x_{S},x_{S^{c}})}{f_{S}(x_{S})}+a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S}^{\prime},x_{S^{c}})\frac{f(x_{S},x_{S^{c}})-f(x_{S}^{\prime},x_{S^{c}})}{f_{S}(x_{S})}

\displaystyle\hskip 175.0pt+a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S}^{\prime},x_{S^{c}})f(x_{S}^{\prime},x_{S^{c}})\biggl\{\frac{1}{f_{S}(x_{S})}-\frac{1}{f_{S}(x_{S}^{\prime})}\biggr\}\biggr]\,dx_{S^{c}}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x_{S^{c}}\in[0,1]^{S^{c}}}|a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S},x_{S^{c}})-a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x_{S}^{\prime},x_{S^{c}})|+4(L_{2}/c_{0})\|a\|_{\infty}\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}.

(90)

It follows inductively, using (
13
) for the
m=0
case, that

\displaystyle|a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x)-a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq L_{1}\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta_{1}}+4m(L_{2}/c_{0})\|a\|_{\infty}\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}.

(91)

For
\delta\in[0,1/2]
write
\partial_{\delta}=[0,1]^{d}\setminus[\delta,1-\delta]^{d}
for all those points within distance
\delta
of the boundary of our sample space. In the following we make repeated use of the fact that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
,
S\in\mathbb{S}\setminus\{S_{m}\}
and
x\in[0,1]^{d}
we have

\bar{k}_{h}^{(\mathbf{S},S)}(x)=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}-1\biggr\}\biggr]=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}\biggr\}\biggr].

First, we see inductively that for any
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
we have
\|\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}\|_{\infty}\leq(2^{d}C_{0}/c_{0})^{m}\|a\|_{\infty}
. We will use this simple bound when
x\in\partial_{mh}
, where it is more difficult to control the difference between
\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}
and
a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}
. For
x\in[0,1]^{d}
and
S\in\mathbb{S}
define

\epsilon_{S}(x)=\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}-1\biggr\}=\mathbb{E}\biggl\{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)\biggl(\frac{1}{f_{S,h}(X)}-\frac{1}{f_{S}(X)}\biggr)\biggr\}.

Using the lower bound
f_{S,h}(x)\geq 2^{-d}c_{0}
and the standard bound
|f_{S,h}(x)-f_{S}(x)|\leq L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}}
when
x\in[h/2,1-h/2]^{d}
, we have

|\epsilon_{S}(x)|\leq\frac{2^{d}}{c_{0}}(L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\not\in\partial_{h}\}}+C_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x\in\partial_{h}\}}).

(92)

Our claim, which we prove inductively, is that whenever
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
and
x\in[mh,1-mh]^{d}
we have

|\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(x)-a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(x)|\leq\binom{m+2}{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{m}(C_{0}L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+\|a\|_{\infty}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}}).

(93)

The
m=1
case follows from (
91
) and (
92
) on writing

\displaystyle|\bar{k}_{h}^{S}(x)-a_{S}^{(1)}(x)|=\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl\{a_{S}(X)\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}\biggr\}-a_{S}(x)]\biggr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl\{a_{S}(X)-a_{S}(x)\bigr\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}\biggr]+\epsilon_{S}(x)a_{S}(x)]\biggr|\leq\frac{2^{d}}{c_{0}}(C_{0}L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+\|a\|_{\infty}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}}).

Now, for the induction step, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{S}^{(m)}
,
S\in\mathbb{S}\setminus\{S_{m}\}
and
x\in[(m+1)h,1-(m+1)h]^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\bar{k}_{h}^{(\mathbf{S},S)}(x)-a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x)\bigr|=\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}-1\biggr\}\biggr]-a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x)\biggr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl\{\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)-a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\bigr\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}+\bigl\{a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)-a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x)\bigr\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}

\displaystyle\hskip 250.0pt+a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x)\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}-1\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl\{\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)-a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\bigr\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}+\bigl\{a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(X)-a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x)\bigr\}\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 250.0pt+a_{(\mathbf{S},S)}^{(m+1)}(x)\epsilon_{S}(x)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\binom{m+2}{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{m}(C_{0}L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+\|a\|_{\infty}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}})\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\{L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+4(m+1)\|a\|_{\infty}(L_{2}/c_{0})h^{\beta_{2}}\}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{K_{h}^{S}(x-X)}{f_{S,h}(X)}\biggr\}+2\|a\|_{\infty}\frac{2^{d}}{c_{0}}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\binom{m+2}{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{m+1}(C_{0}L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+\|a\|_{\infty}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}})+\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{2}\{L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+(m+2)\|a\|_{\infty}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\binom{m+3}{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{m+1}(C_{0}L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+\|a\|_{\infty}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}}),

proving the claim (
93
). It now follows from (
93
) and our uniform bound on
\|\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}\|_{\infty}
that we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\{\bar{k}_{h}^{\mathbf{S}}(X)-a_{\mathbf{S}}^{(m)}(X)\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{\binom{m+2}{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{m}(C_{0}L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+\|a\|_{\infty}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}})\biggr\}^{2}+\mathbb{P}(X\in\delta_{mh})\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{m}+2\biggr\}^{2}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\binom{m+2}{2}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{2m}(C_{0}L_{1}h^{\beta_{1}}+\|a\|_{\infty}L_{2}h^{\beta_{2}})^{2}+2dmhC_{0}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{m}+2\biggr\}^{2}\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\binom{m+2}{2}^{2}\biggl(\frac{2^{d}C_{0}}{c_{0}}\biggr)^{2m}\{C_{0}^{2}L_{1}^{2}h^{2{\beta_{1}}}+(L_{2}^{2}h^{2\beta_{2}}+C_{0}dh)\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}\}.

The result now follows from (
89
).

∎

Proof of Corollary
4
.

Using the notation of Section
4
, let
h,T,M
be chosen such that

A^{2M}\{MT^{d}/(Nh^{d})+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\}\rightarrow 0

(94)

and
T,M\rightarrow\infty
. Writing
N_{[d]}=N-\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}N_{S}
, let
E=\{N_{[d]}\geq 2M,N_{S}\geq 1\,\forall S\in\mathbb{S}\}
be the event that our random sample sizes are sufficiently large that our fixed-sample-size estimator can be applied. To emphasise the dependence of this estimator on the sample sizes we write
\widehat{\theta}\equiv\widehat{\theta}(n_{\mathbb{S}})
. We take our estimator in the current setting to be

\widehat{\Theta}=\mathbbm{1}_{E}\widehat{\theta}(N_{\mathbb{S}}).

Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
15
, on the event
E
, we have almost surely that

\displaystyle N\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{\theta}(N_{\mathbb{S}})-\theta\bigr\}^{2}\bigm|N_{\mathbb{S}}\bigr]-\frac{N}{N_{[d]}}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N}{N_{[d]}}\biggl(1+\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl|\frac{N_{[d]}\lambda_{S}}{N_{S}}-1\biggr|\biggr)\biggl[4\kappa\exp(-M/\kappa)\mathrm{Var}\,a(X)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+200|\mathbb{S}|^{2}(C/c)\biggl(1+\frac{N}{\min_{S}N_{S}}\biggr)A^{M}B\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{N_{[d]}h^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\max\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{N}{N_{[d]}}\biggr)^{3/2},1\biggr\}\biggl(1+\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl|\frac{N_{[d]}\lambda_{S}}{N_{S}}-1\biggr|\biggr)\biggl(1+\frac{N}{\min_{S}N_{S}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.0pt\times\biggl[4\kappa\exp(-M/\kappa)\mathrm{Var}\,a(X)+200|\mathbb{S}|^{2}(C/c)A^{M}B\biggl\{\frac{MT^{d}}{Nh^{d}}+h^{2\beta_{\wedge}}+\mathbb{P}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq T)\biggr\}^{1/2}\biggr],

where we see that the final factor inside square brackets is deterministic and converges to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Denoting this by
\delta_{N}
we may now write

\displaystyle N\mathbb{E}\bigl[

\displaystyle\bigl\{\widehat{\theta}(N_{\mathbb{S}})-\theta\bigr\}^{2}\bigr]-\frac{1}{p_{[d]}}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})=N\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\{\widehat{\theta}(N_{\mathbb{S}})-\theta\bigr\}^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{E}\bigr]-\frac{1}{p_{[d]}}\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})+N\theta^{2}\mathbb{P}(E^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(\alpha_{\mathbb{S}}^{*})\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\mathbbm{1}_{E}\biggl(\frac{N}{N_{[d]}}-\frac{1}{p_{[d]}}\biggr)\biggr\}+\|a\|_{\infty}^{2}N\mathbb{P}(E^{c})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\delta_{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathbbm{1}_{E}\max\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{N}{N_{[d]}}\biggr)^{3/2},1\biggr\}\biggl(1+\max_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\biggl|\frac{N_{[d]}\lambda_{S}}{N_{S}}-1\biggr|\biggr)\biggl(1+\frac{N}{\min_{S}N_{S}}\biggr)\biggr].

It now suffices to show that each of these three terms converges to zero. For the first term, we see that
\mathbbm{1}_{E}N/N_{[d]}
is uniformly integrable by writing

\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\mathbbm{1}_{E}\frac{N}{N_{[d]}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{N\geq KN_{[d]}\}}\biggr\}\leq N\mathbb{P}(N_{[d]}/N\leq 1/K),

and using Hoeffding’s inequality to see that this can be made arbitrarily small, uniformly in
N
by choosing
K
large enough. Thus, since
N/N_{[d}
converges in probability to
1/p_{[d]}
we may conclude that

\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\mathbbm{1}_{E}\biggl(\frac{N}{N_{[d]}}-\frac{1}{p_{[d]}}\biggr)\biggr\}\rightarrow 0.

Very similar arguments can be used to show that the third term also converges to zero. Finally, since (
94
) implies that
M\leq Np_{[d]}/4
for
N
sufficiently large, we have that

\displaystyle N\mathbb{P}(E^{c})

\displaystyle\leq N\sum_{S\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{P}(N_{S}=0)+N\mathbb{P}(N_{[d]}<Np_{[d]}/2)

\displaystyle\leq N|\mathbb{S}|\exp(-N\min_{S\in\mathbb{S}}p_{S})+N\exp(-Np_{[d]}^{2}/2)\rightarrow 0,

where we use Hoeffding’s inequality for the final bound.
∎
